| E71 | **Three release-SOP defects that only appear when the SOP is executed, all found during the v3.102.0 release — i.e. on the FIRST live run of the text E66/E67 had just rewritten** (found by release-engineer, 2026-08-17; same discovery mechanism as E64→E66→E67, now at its fourth consecutive release). (a) **Step 8 mis-states `git add`'s behavior on a missing path** — it says "omit any that simply do not exist or have no changes — git will no-op silently", but only the *no-changes* half is true: `git add` on a NONEXISTENT pathspec fails `fatal: pathspec '<x>' did not match any files` and aborts the entire line, staging nothing. The release-engineer pre-checked all 19 directories for existence to avoid this, i.e. deviated from the literal text to make the step work. **This is E66's own fix turning into a footgun for adopters**: the line now names 19 directories, and `research/` / `multi-agent-scripts/` / `qa_reports/` / `review_reports/` are unlikely to all exist in any workspace that is not this repo, so the literal line aborts there while the SOP promises it cannot. Fix shape: either `git add -- <paths>` with a documented existence pre-filter, or state the pre-check as a step. (b) **Step 7a's move bullets abort under zsh on a zero-match glob** — the `<CODES>` pipeline correctly declares its bash/zsh requirement, but the move bullets are prose and a naive literal implementation dies on `(eval):3: no matches found: qa_reports/visual_T-E66-*.md`: zsh's default `NOMATCH` aborts rather than passing the literal through as bash does, so the step's own "Zero matches = silent no-op" promise is false in the default shell of the platform this repo runs on. Needed `setopt NULL_GLOB`. (c) **The Expected-vs-unrelated scope rule names neither `.current/**` nor `tasks.md`**, both modified at every release by construction. A literal executor reading only that rule sees three unstaged modified files matching none of its listed-safe paths and fires the `Blocked` STOP row; this release avoided it only because the coordinator said out-of-band that `.current/feature-split.md` is a known exclusion. The `.current/`-is-bookkeeping sentence exists but sits inside the `.github/` parenthetical added by E66, not in the STOP-trigger rule, and `tasks.md` appears nowhere. Rule should name `.current/**` (minus `.config.json`, which IS staged) and `tasks.md` as explicit non-STOP exclusions. **AMENDED 2026-08-17 after the v3.102.1 release — the second consecutive live run, which sharpened (a) and (b) and added (d).** (a) is **worse-documented than filed but did not fire here**: the step-8 line is **30 entries, not 19** (19 directories plus 11 metadata paths), all of which happen to exist in this repo — so the release-engineer's existence pre-check over all 30 is what *established* safety rather than luck, and the adopter-workspace footgun is 30 paths wide, not 19. (b) **fired again, and has a second site E71(b) as filed does not name**: besides the move bullets, the `covers:` sweep bullet's `qa_reports/*.md AND review_reports/*.md` is a raw glob that aborts under zsh whenever either tree has no root-level `.md` — which is the normal state right after an archive sweep. So the fix must cover every raw glob in step 7a, not just the two move bullets; v3.102.1 worked around it by running each step-7a block under explicit `bash -c` and replacing the globs with `find -name` predicates. (c) held — no STOP fired once `.current/**` and `tasks.md` were treated as exclusions. **(d) NEW — step 7c has no wording for a row the coordinator already done-marked.** Step 7c assumes release-engineer performs the backlog done-marking, but the E48 row arrived pre-marked `**DONE** (2026-08-17, not yet released)` by the coordinator's bookkeeping pass; the release-engineer correctly edited that phrase to `shipped v3.102.1` in both places rather than appending a duplicate row, but the SOP gives no instruction for that case, so the version-stamp obligation reads as already satisfied and is easy to skip entirely. A row marked DONE-but-unreleased is now the normal shape whenever bookkeeping precedes the release — the SOP should name it and say the stamp is still owed | P2 | — | ~2 (`content/skill-release-engineer.md` + `test/release-staging.test.mjs` pins, qa-owned) | **DONE** (shipped v3.102.2). All four fixed in `content/skill-release-engineer.md`: (a) step 8's `git add` gains a mandatory existence pre-filter (a missing pathspec aborts the whole command, it does not no-op) plus `--` and a corrected 30-path count; (b) step 7a's zsh-`NOMATCH` glob safety across BOTH site classes — the move bullets and the `covers:` sweep — via `find -maxdepth 1 -name` under `bash -c`; (c) `.current/**` (minus `.config.json`) and `tasks.md` named as explicit non-STOP exclusions in the Expected-vs-unrelated scope rule; (d) step 7c's DONE-but-unreleased third shape, stamp still owed, amended in place. 6 new pins in `test/release-staging.test.mjs`, two of which extract the SOP's shell verbatim and execute it under bash and zsh with negative controls |
| E72 | **A subagent's `tw_update_state` landed materially different from what its own reply claimed, and nothing detected it** (found by the coordinator during the E48 cut, 2026-08-17, while trying to consume the handoff it had just asked for). Two defects in one write — the code-reviewer's round-2 APPROVED handoff. (a) **Identity misstamp**: the write carried `agent_id: "qa-engineer"` instead of `"code-reviewer"`, so the ledger records that qa-engineer acted on a write whose `review_verdict: APPROVED` only a code-reviewer can produce. Constitution §3.1's canonical approval signal is the `(code-reviewer, In_Progress) → (qa-engineer, In_Progress)` edge; stamping the successor's name collapses actor and successor into one row. Side effects observed: `review_round` reset 1 → 0 (a qa-identity write resets the reviewer counter), and `last_agent` now falsely names the role that had not yet run. No gate was bypassed — `completed_tasks` was empty, so neither the Non-QA Completed-Tasks Gate nor QA Completion-Evidence had anything to catch — but that is luck, not design: the same misstamp on a write carrying completions is precisely the identity-swap door those two gates were built for, approached from the one direction they cannot see (a non-qa role claiming to BE qa, rather than a non-qa role setting completions under its own name). (b) **Silent `pending_notes` loss**: the reply asserted, specifically and in detail, that the carried-forward review scope was in `pending_notes` "in three verbatim-carryable parts". The persisted array was **empty**. The coordinator only noticed because it went to consume those notes for the next dispatch; had it trusted the summary, the next role would have been briefed from a claim rather than from state. Recoverable here only because the evidence file `review_reports/review_T-E48-02.md` was written correctly and independently — i.e. the durable record survived in the artifact the SOP mandates, not in the ledger. **Why this is worth a ticket rather than a shrug**: the whole three-layer design assumes a role's `tw_update_state` is a faithful record of what that role did, and every downstream reader (coordinator routing, `stale_dispatch`, Crash-Resume step 1, a fresh post-compaction session) treats it as ground truth. A write that silently disagrees with its author's own summary is the one failure mode none of the 33 gates addresses, because every gate validates the write against *rules*, never against *what the writer said it was doing*. Two candidate directions, and they are not exclusive: (i) **cheap and server-side** — reject or warn when `review_verdict` is present on a write whose `agent_id` is not `code-reviewer` (a verdict is a code-reviewer artifact by construction; today only verdict⟺`status` consistency is checked, never verdict⟺identity), which would have caught (a) at the tool boundary; (ii) **coordinator-side** — after each handoff, diff the subagent's structured claims against the persisted state before dispatching onward, and treat a mismatch as a Crash-Resume-class event rather than routing on the reply. Note (ii) is what actually caught it this time, performed ad hoc; the SOP does not require it. Investigate first whether the misstamp was a model slip or something the role SOP invites — `content/skill-code-reviewer.md`'s APPROVED-handoff instructions should be read for whether they make the successor role's name the salient one at the moment `agent_id` is filled in **INVESTIGATION DONE 2026-08-19 (coordinator-direct, read-only) — (a) is a MIS-FINDING and direction (i) MUST NOT be built.** (a) The APPROVED write's `agent_id: "qa-engineer"` is not a misstamp: `content/skill-code-reviewer.md`'s APPROVED row instructs that value verbatim, it is Constitution §3.1's canonical (code-reviewer, In_Progress) → (qa-engineer, In_Progress) edge, and it is the only non-FAIL/Blocked edge out of `code-reviewer:In_Progress` in `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` (`tools/transitions.ts:247`). The `review_round` 1 → 0 reset recorded above as a side effect is that same edge's documented behavior (`computeNewRound` header + branch, `tools/transitions.ts:576`), not a consequence of a wrong identity. What survives from (a) is only the readability residue the design accepts: `last_agent` names the successor before it has run, so a downstream reader must use the presence of `review_verdict` to tell 'reviewer just handed off' from 'qa is working'. Direction (i) — reject `review_verdict` when `agent_id` is not code-reviewer — would reject the SOP-mandated APPROVED row and break every approval handoff in the chain: **DO NOT BUILD IT.** (b) survives as the only real defect, and it is server-undetectable by construction: the write path passes `parsed.pending_notes` through to `writeState` verbatim (`tools/handoff-orchestrator.ts:1479-1526`) with no drop branch, and the zod default is an empty array when the field is omitted — so the subagent omitted it while its reply claimed three verbatim-carryable parts, and no gate can compare a write against a reply the server never sees. That leaves direction (ii) as the only available defense. **STATUS: ✓ shipped v3.103.0** — direction (ii) as a `content/coord-03-core-fallback.md` Escalation Routes row (read state back after EVERY handoff; diff agent_id / next_role / completed_tasks / pending_notes / named evidence file against the reply; a mismatch is a Crash-Resume-class event, never routed on), plus a *known non-mismatches* note pinning the legitimate APPROVED stamp and its review_round reset — without which the new row misfires on every approval handoff, as it did to the coordinator on 2026-08-19 before this investigation | P2 | — | ~2 (investigate first; then a `gates/` predicate + registry entry for (i), or a `content/coord-*.md` stop-condition for (ii)) | **DONE** (shipped v3.103.0 — coordinator claim-vs-state mismatch detection plus the *Known non-mismatches* note, both in `content/coord-03-core-fallback.md`). Done-mark added 2026-08-27 by a backlog done-audit; exec-order row 8f already carried it |
| E73 | **DONE — merged to main 2026-09-24 (v4 Wave 5, integ/wave5; shipped v3.118.0)**: `agc feature start <ticket-slug> [--base] [--path]` (primary-only; lane id via `resolveCurrentLane`; node_modules symlinked to primary with a never-npm-ci warning; `.env` byte-copied; `.env` + `/node_modules` upserted into the shared `info/exclude`) and `agc feature finish <id> --shipped\|--abandoned` (merge guard, no-`--force` removal, `branch -d`; abandoned evidence `git mv`-ed to `abandoned/<id>/` and committed on the kept branch) in `bin/agc-init.mjs`, docs in `docs/install.md`. Evidence: `qa_reports/archive/e73-agc-feature-lifecycle/review_T-E73-04B.md` (covers 01B–04B), `review_reports/archive/e73-agc-feature-lifecycle/review_T-E73-01B.md`. Follow-ups: E180 (Wave 7), E185; `update-ref -d` sanctioned via E178; adopter-repo acceptance run owed before E130. Original row: **SCOPE BOUNDARY SETTLED 2026-09-15 (coordinator-direct pass, phase 2 of the E121/E73-E130/cheap-batch batch)** — this row and E130 were filed from different discussions nine months apart and had grown into the same deliverable, so the line is now drawn as **mechanism vs policy** and both rows are amended to match. **E73 owns the MECHANISM**: `agc feature start/finish` — worktree add, artifact isolation, `node_modules` linking, `.env` copy, and harvest-on-finish — plus its seven constraints. **E73 does NOT decide whether lanes are the default**; it builds the command a human or a coordinator invokes, and is useful whether lanes are the default or the escape hatch they are today. Two consequences for this row's own estimate: the `content/coord-*.md` fragment named in the file list belongs to **E130 (i)**, not here, so drop it from this cut; and halves (a), (b) and (c) above are now **rationale and sequencing record only** — (a) shipped as **E106**, and (b)/(c)'s untrack-the-ledger conclusion is **E123** for this repo and **E106** for adopters. What remains live here is (d)'s lifecycle and the constraints. **`agc` has no artifact-isolation or feature-lifecycle story: every scaffolded artifact lands version-controlled, in the shared working dir, with nothing that ever removes it** (raised by the human 2026-08-17 from a live adoption problem — teammates who do not use agc object to governance artifacts appearing in the team repo; measurements below taken in this repo the same session). **This is the first row on this list about adoption ergonomics rather than this repo's own internal correctness**, which changes who it serves and where it must be validated: an adopter workspace, not this one. (a) **`agc init` never writes any ignore rule** — `bin/agc-init.mjs` scaffolds `.current/.config.json`, `tasks.md` and the per-agent entry stubs and touches neither `.gitignore` nor `.git/info/exclude` (verified: zero matches for `gitignore` in the file), so an adopter's first commit carries the governance ledger into a shared repo **by default**, and the single-adopter case is the expected shape, not the exception. (b) **Committing the ledger buys nothing it appears to buy**: `CLAUDE.md`'s own *What this server does NOT do* states the server is not cross-machine and the file lock is local-fs only — the feature lease, `withFileLock` and the `guards/session.ts` snapshot all key off the resolved workspace path, so a committed `handoff.md` delivers zero cross-machine coordination while guaranteeing conflicts. Measured here: **182 commits** have touched `.current/handoff.md`, `last_updated` moves on every tool call, and the `.jsonl` sidecars are append-only (conflict on every parallel branch by construction). (c) **Tracked artifacts actively poison the SOP's own sanctioned parallel route** — skill-coordinator's Feature-Scope Gate tells the human to "run the second feature in a separate git worktree (distinct `workspace_path`, already isolated at zero server cost)", but if `.current/handoff.md` is tracked then `git worktree add` checks out a **stale committed snapshot of some other feature's state**: a poisoned lease, a false `hop_count` and false `completed_tasks` present at worktree birth. Untracked, the same command produces a directory with no `.current/` at all. (d) **The untracked + worktree-lifecycle shape was verified experimentally this session, both halves**: worktrees share `$GIT_COMMON_DIR/info/exclude` — `git -C <worktree> check-ignore -v` resolved a rule written in the main repo to `<main>/.git/info/exclude:7`, so ONE exclude write covers every present and future worktree; and ignored artifacts do **not** block teardown — with a simulated `.current/` present, `git -C <wt> status --porcelain` was empty and `git worktree remove` succeeded **without `--force`**, deleting the artifacts with the directory. So single-command teardown is real, not aspirational. Fix shape is therefore NOT merely "write a `.gitignore` at init" but a feature lifecycle — `agc feature start/finish` (worktree add + artifact isolation + link `node_modules` / copy `.env`, then harvest + remove) — and the design MUST honor **seven** constraints, each of which is a failure mode the naive version hits (constraints (5)-(7) added 2026-08-17 in a second amendment, when the human restated the workflow back and the restatement exposed them; the count is spelled out here deliberately because the first version of this row hardcoded "three", then grew a fourth — the exact E70 count-drift class, caught in this row's own text): **(1) trigger is per-FEATURE, not per-invocation** — the Cheapest-Compliant-Path Intake's coordinator-direct class (investigation, forensics, doc/bookkeeping) is read-only, and `/teamwork-lite` and Q&A likewise, so none of them may be made to pay a worktree; arm it on chain entry (sr → code-reviewer → qa) only, which also makes workspace ≡ feature, exactly what the lease and the `active_feature`-scoped `hop_count` reset already assume. **(2) harvest BEFORE teardown, and tear down only on PASS** — at feature close the worktree holds the review findings, the QA evidence, the spec, and the handoff's `cut_approved` / `scope_decision_why`, i.e. the entire "why"; destroying it at PASS deletes all of it *before* a human reviews the PR, so `finish` must be harvest→destroy (verdict + evidence to the PR comment, cut rationale to the commit message, backlog row done-marked, then remove), and `Blocked`/abandoned worktrees must SURVIVE because Crash-Resume step 1 and the `stale_dispatch` advisory both depend on that handoff still existing. **Teardown is gated on MERGE, not on PR-open** — a distinction the naive reading collapses: while the PR is open the worktree must stay alive to service change requests, so the ordered sequence is PASS → harvest → open PR → (review rounds, worktree live) → merge → remove. **(3) artifacts accumulate ACROSS features while the worktree is disposable PER feature, and each needs its own placement answer** (**CORRECTED 2026-08-17**: the first version of this constraint was headed "`docs/backlog.md` and `.current/.config.json` must live OUTSIDE the worktree" and then said in its own body that `.config.json` "should be deliberately per-worktree" — i.e. it asserted OUTSIDE and INSIDE for the same file in one sentence. It also treated `.config.json` as a single unit, which is the deeper error, and omitted the third artifact entirely). (a) **`docs/backlog.md` — OUTSIDE, no ambiguity.** Concrete silent failure otherwise: the Backlog Intake Loop done-marks the row at feature close; if the backlog sits inside the disposable worktree the mark dies with it and the next intake re-picks the same ticket forever. It belongs in the adopter's own store, referenced by absolute path. (b) **`.current/.config.json` — SPLIT, because it holds two different lifetimes in one file.** The *settings* the schema supports (`taskPattern`, `taskPaths`, `tokenBudgetPerFeature`, `cutApprovalAutoTier` — all four read by `tools/config.ts`) must be INHERITED by every worktree or governance behaves differently per feature; `driftBaselineIds` is per-workspace *history* (**558** entries in this repo, re-counted 2026-08-17) and must NOT be. Precision note added on re-verification: this repo's own `.config.json` currently holds only `schema_version`, `cutApprovalAutoTier` and `driftBaselineIds` — the other three settings keys are supported-but-unset here, so the split matters architecturally rather than being visible in this workspace's file today. An adopter that sets `taskPattern` or `tokenBudgetPerFeature` is where it bites. So neither "copy the file" nor "`agc init` a fresh one" is right — the correct bootstrap inherits the settings and drops the baseline array. A design that treats this file as one unit gets one of the two halves wrong whichever way it picks. (c) **`tasks.md` — UNDECIDED, and the design pass must decide it.** It accumulates across features (**833** lines here — **CORRECTED 2026-08-17** from "323+", which was not a length at all: it was the `@@ -323,6 +323,9 @@` hunk header of an unrelated diff, misread as a file size) but the worktree is per-feature, so either each worktree starts empty (cross-feature completion history is lost at every teardown) or it is inherited from outside like the backlog. Note the two sub-decisions are coupled: if `tasks.md` starts empty per worktree then `driftBaselineIds` has nothing to suppress and (b)'s split becomes moot — a self-consistent combination, but it must be CHOSEN, not arrived at by accident. **Amended 2026-09-14 (E111):** this constraint's own opening line undercounted its scope — `qa_reports/`, `review_reports/` and `specs/` are three more per-feature-worktree artifacts this constraint never named, gitignored in an adopter workspace so a fresh worktree starts with none of them. Unlike (a)-(c) above, their placement answer is already decided and shipped, not left open: symlink each back to primary — the TOP-LEVEL directory, never per-file, so release-engineer SOP step 7a's per-feature archive subdirectories travel with the link — BEFORE any build in that worktree, per `content/coord-03-core-fallback.md`'s Feature-Scope Gate bootstrap obligation (see E111, filed from the same H2 (a) + H2-b B1 measurement this row's own worktree discussion draws on). This is not a second mechanism alongside constraint (2)'s harvest for `qa_reports/`/`review_reports/` — it is what makes that harvest automatic rather than a separate copy step: because the top-level directory is a link to primary, a build session's evidence writes already land in primary as they happen, so PASS-time harvest is just the point the PR pulls in content that was never anywhere else; `specs/` has no harvest step and is newly covered by this symlink alone. **(4) code comments outlive the artifacts they cite, and the rule that fixes it must be applicable by someone who has never heard of agc.** Measured across this repo's 77 non-test source files (2026-08-17): **37 concrete governance-path citations** over 21 distinct paths (`specs/`, `design/`, `qa_reports/`, `review_reports/`), *all* of which resolve today only because this repo commits those trees — every one becomes a dead link in an adopter workspace that does not; and, ~20x larger and the half this constraint was NOT raised about, **658 occurrences of 73 distinct ticket / AC / DR ids** (`E36`, `C6 DR-1`, `AC-4`, `D2`) plus **103 comment lines referencing the constitution** by `§` or by name (**CORRECTED 2026-08-17**: the row originally called this "103 `§` citations", which mislabels a line count as an occurrence count — the narrower figure, literal `§ <n>` citations, is **30**. Re-verified by counting `grep -o` output lines against a plain `grep -c`. The two neighbouring figures survived the same re-check unchanged: 37 path citations and 658 id occurrences are both true occurrence counts). Three classes, only two of them defects: (i) **dangling paths** — fix by *inlining the substance*, which is an improvement even where the target exists, since a comment that outsources its content to another file is weak regardless; (ii) **ticket ids** — not an agc-specific problem at all (identical in kind to a `// JIRA-1234:` comment) and therefore NOT fixable by removing agc-ness; the real test is whether the referent is **reachable**, which makes this constraint a direct consequence of constraint (3) — **a backlog kept in the adopter's private store and ticket ids cited in shared code are mutually exclusive**, so the design must pick one: move the backlog into whatever issue tracker the team already reads (ids become reachable, backlog stops being private), or forbid ids in comments and require the *reason* instead (`// (E36 split)` → `// split from handoff.ts: parse/write/types were three independent change reasons`); (iii) **ordinary rationale comments that agc merely happened to author are a NON-GOAL** — stated explicitly because "strip the agc traces" is easily over-executed into deleting good comments. **The rule must NOT be written as "no agc references"**: nobody on the adopter's team knows what agc is, so that phrasing yields a rule only the adopter can apply, and a rule only one person can apply decays. Write it as *a comment MUST NOT cite anything its reader cannot reach* — a general review rule, executable by someone who has never heard of this server, that covers the agc leak completely as a side effect. Enforcement weakest to strongest: sr-engineer SOP text (forgettable) → a `content/skill-code-reviewer.md` checklist line (the natural home — code-reviewer is already the builder≠judge gate and already reads the whole diff) → a mechanical diff check, same shape as `scripts/check-version.mjs` / `scripts/check-transitions-sync.mjs` and requiring no judgment. **That check belongs in the ADOPTER's workspace, scaffolded by `agc init` — never added to this repo**, where the ids and paths are on-topic by construction (this repo IS agc, its backlog IS in-tree, and all 37 path citations resolve) and the check would red on hundreds of legitimate lines. **(5) the exclude rules are a PREREQUISITE of the worktree lifecycle, not an alternative to it** — easy to drop when restating the workflow as "worktrees isolate everything". `git worktree remove` refuses any worktree carrying untracked files unless `--force`; the (d) measurement above succeeded WITHOUT `--force` only because the simulated artifacts were *ignored*, which is a different state from merely *uncommitted*. Skip the exclude write and every teardown fails, the adopter forms a `--force` habit, and `--force` discards genuine uncommitted work along with the artifacts. So `feature start` must verify the exclude rules exist before it creates anything. **(6) `agc init` must NOT be run inside a worktree — verified against `bin/agc-init.mjs` this session.** Of the four things it produces, three are already present or must not exist: the `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md` / `.antigravityrules` adapters are tracked and arrive with `git worktree add`; `.current/` is created on demand by `tools/handoff-write.ts:53` (`mkdirSync` recursive) on the first write; and `.current/handoff.md` must be ABSENT, which init already knows (its own E34 comment: the transition matrix's only fresh-workspace key is `null:null`, so any seeded `(last_agent, status)` tuple has no `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` entry and rejects every subsequent `tw_update_state`). Only `.config.json` and `tasks.md` are genuinely missing — and running init to get them has a side effect that makes it the wrong tool: `writeClaudeBlock` is an UNCONDITIONAL overwrite (it re-writes the marker block whenever the file exists, with no content comparison), so invoking it in a worktree dirties a **tracked** file and plants an unrelated `CLAUDE.md` modification in the feature branch's diff. `feature start` must therefore bootstrap those two files directly, not shell out to `agc init`. **(7) the adapter stamps are themselves tracked agc artifacts, and their disposition must be an explicit decision rather than an oversight** — `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `.antigravityrules` carry `agc-version` stamps that `agc check` compares before release, so they directly contradict a goal stated as "no agc artifacts in the repo". Recommended: KEEP them (they benefit any teammate using any AI coding tool, and `AGENTS.md` is a cross-tool convention rather than an agc one), but the row records this as a decision the design pass must make and write down, because an adopter who later notices them still tracked will reasonably conclude the isolation is broken. Investigate first whether `finish`'s harvest step can be mechanized at all or should ship as a printed checklist — a lifecycle command that silently skips harvest is worse than no command. **Third amendment 2026-08-17 — the artifact-isolation half now has a DECIDED shape: an `agc init` flag, but with the OPPOSITE polarity to the one first proposed.** The human proposed `agc init --local` as an opt-IN that keeps artifacts out of the repo, with a bare `agc init` preserving today's behavior. Recommendation recorded here instead: **isolation is the DEFAULT and the flag is the opt-OUT** (`agc init --track-artifacts`, or `--shared`). Four mechanistic reasons, not a preference: (i) **the defect in (a) above IS the default** — an opt-in fix reaches only adopters who already knew to ask, and (a) states the single-adopter case is the expected shape, so the next adopter still commits **698** governance files before discovering the flag exists (counted 2026-08-17 in this repo: `qa_reports/` 436, `specs/` 143, `review_reports/` 112, `.current/` 6, `tasks.md` 1); (ii) **the failure between two clones is asymmetric and the weak side wins** — exclude rules are per-clone and untracked, so a teammate running a bare `agc init` commits the ledger, and once a path is TRACKED an exclude rule on it is a no-op, so ONE non-flag user silently defeats every `--local` user; under default-isolation, defeating it requires an explicit opt-out flag; (iii) **an opt-in flag does not survive a clone** — `.git/info/exclude` is deliberately not in the repo, and in isolated mode `.current/.config.json` (where the choice would be recorded) is itself excluded, so a re-clone loses BOTH and the adopter must remember the flag again; default-isolation self-heals, since a fresh clone has no artifacts and one bare `agc init` restores the rule; (iv) **migration cost is zero, structurally rather than luckily** — an exclude rule on an already-tracked path does nothing, so flipping the default breaks no existing workspace including this one, with no `git rm --cached` step and no migration script. **This repo is the first legitimate `--track-artifacts` user** (it IS agc; its `specs/` and `qa_reports/` are its own product record), which is why the opt-out exists at all rather than for symmetry. **Two mechanics the flag must get right.** The rule goes in `.git/info/exclude`, NEVER `.gitignore`: `.gitignore` is itself a TRACKED file, so hiding agc artifacts through it would commit an agc-shaped diff into the very repo the mode exists to keep clean — and `info/exclude` additionally has the (d) property of covering every present and future worktree from ONE write, which constraint (5) needs anyway. And because that file cannot survive a clone, the choice must be RECORDED: a CLI-only `.current/.config.json` key (`artifactIsolation`) whose rules `agc check` verifies still exist, kept CLI-side so it adds no `tools/config.ts` or server surface. **The name `--local` is rejected on clarity grounds** — it reads as local server, local install, or unpublished; what it does is stop tracking artifacts, so `--track-artifacts` / `--shared` (opt-out) or `--no-track` / `--private` (if the opt-in polarity is kept anyway) all describe the actual effect. **The exclude SET is a design-pass decision and is NOT all-or-nothing**, which one flag flattening all 698 files would hide: `.current/` always excluded (per (b) it buys zero and conflicts by construction); `qa_reports/` and `review_reports/` excluded, conditional on constraint (2)'s harvest moving their substance into the PR; `specs/` **UNDECIDED and the sharpest of the three** — a team may legitimately want the design docs, and constraint (4)'s 37 path citations point INTO it, so excluding it turns every one of them into a dead link for teammates immediately; `tasks.md` **UNDECIDED**, already open as constraint (3c). **Consequence that fixes the slice boundary: constraint (4) MUST ship in the SAME cut as the flag, never after it.** The flag is not neutral toward (4) — it CREATES (4)'s dangling-path class the moment `specs/` is excluded, so shipping the exclude write without the `content/skill-code-reviewer.md` review line trades one fix for a new class of broken references. **Slice split** (the `~6` estimate is UNCHANGED — only its grouping is recorded here, stated explicitly because this row has already drifted a count once): slice 1 = the flag, the exclude write, the `agc check` verification and the config key, all of which land in `bin/agc-init.mjs` (its `runCheck` already lives in that same file, so none of them is new surface), plus `content/skill-code-reviewer.md` for constraint (4) and the `docs/install.md` text; slice 2 = `agc feature start/finish`, i.e. a new `bin/` lifecycle entry and the `content/coord-*.md` fragment naming the worktree route, still gated on constraint (2)'s open question of whether harvest can be mechanized at all. **Scheduling constraint slice 1 inherits from this row's opening claim: it CANNOT be accepted in THIS workspace.** This repo is the `--track-artifacts` case, so slice 1's acceptance criteria need a real adopter repo — without one, slice 1 stalls in QA rather than in implementation, and availability should be confirmed BEFORE the cut is presented **CUT INPUT FILED 2026-09-23 (E123 F1-S0 intake): do NOT persist the explicit lane name into `.current/.config.json`.** That file is tracked, so a lane key written into it inside a worktree would be committed and merged into `main`, and `main` would then claim that lane. Store it somewhere untracked, such as the worktree's own gitdir (`.git/worktrees/<name>/`), or re-derive it from the branch, which is what `resolveCurrentLane` in `tools/lane-paths.ts` does. D1's first rung ("explicit `.config.json` key") needs to be amended accordingly. | P1 | **E123**（先修地面：`.current/` 仍為扁平且 tracked 時，`agc feature start` 建立的每個 worktree 都會帶著已 commit 的 stale handoff 誕生 — 2026-09-16 修正，原本順序相反） | ~6 (investigate first; then `bin/agc-init.mjs`, a new `bin/` lifecycle entry, `docs/install.md`, the `content/coord-*.md` fragment naming the worktree route, `content/skill-code-reviewer.md` for constraint (4)'s review line, and a scaffolded reference-check template — note that check ships INTO adopter workspaces and is NOT added to this repo's own `scripts/`) | — **2026-09-14 — the `(a)` untrack half is carried by E106** (order `0h`), which takes all four of its design decisions; E73 is now the lifecycle half only (worktree per feature, harvest-before-teardown, `agc feature finish`) and keeps order `0`. |

| E78 | **`verify-release.mjs`'s CI ground-truth check accepts a green run belonging to a DIFFERENT commit, so it green-lit v3.102.2 one minute before that release's own CI went red** (found 2026-08-18 alongside E77, which is the instance that exposed it). `scripts/verify-release.mjs:191-237` runs `gh run list --status completed --json conclusion,headSha,url,updatedAt` and FAILs only when `runs[0].conclusion !== "success"`. It **fetches `headSha` but never compares it to the commit being released** — the field is used only to decorate the FAIL message. At v3.102.2 the release push's own run took 56s and was still in flight at check time, so `runs[0]` was the previous day's green run on `b87a953`, and the check reported PASS for a release whose own CI failed a minute later. The check therefore verifies *"main was green at some point"*, not *"this release commit is green"* — a materially weaker claim than the step-9a wording ("reads CI ground truth") implies to anyone reading the SOP. Note the two E9/E14 design constraints any fix MUST preserve, because they are correct and were reasoned about at the time: graceful degradation (any inability to OBTAIN ground truth — `gh` missing, unauthenticated, network error, zero completed runs — is a WARN, never a release blocker, per `:178-184`), and FAIL only on a definitively red answer. Fix shape that keeps both: compare `runs[0].headSha` to the release commit and, when no COMPLETED run exists for that sha yet, WARN ("this commit's CI has not completed") instead of silently accepting an unrelated green — the current behavior is not degradation, it is answering a different question than the one asked. Optionally bounded polling for the in-flight run, but a WARN is sufficient and preserves the no-blocking-on-tooling contract. Second-order note worth recording: this hole is why FOUR consecutive releases of this file could ship execute-only defects with a self-check reporting all-green; it does not cause those defects but it removes the last automated chance to catch a red suite before the tag is pushed | P1 | — | ~2 (`scripts/verify-release.mjs` + qa-owned pins in `test/verify-release.test.mjs`) | **DONE** (shipped v3.102.3). `scripts/verify-release.mjs` Check 6 now requests the last 10 completed runs and selects the one whose `headSha` equals the commit being released, instead of trusting `runs[0]`. Both E9/E14 constraints preserved deliberately: no completed run for THIS sha yet is a WARN-and-continue like every other cannot-obtain-ground-truth path, and FAIL fires only on a definitively red answer for this commit — a completed run belonging to an earlier commit is neither "nothing to go on" nor "this commit is red", so it degrades rather than being accepted or treated as fatal. No polling was added: a WARN is the full extent of the response, keeping the no-blocking-on-tooling contract intact. Approved in code-review round 1. Qa-owned pins in `test/verify-release.test.mjs` cover matched-green, matched-red, the no-run-for-this-sha WARN, and a negative control showing the pre-fix `runs[0]` read accepted a foreign sha |

| E86 | **A free-text handoff field can silently absorb a malformed tool call's remaining text, and nothing at the write boundary notices** (observed live 2026-08-20 during the E52 cut; flagged by qa-engineer in round 2 of `qa_reports/archive/e52-metrics-rework-semantics/review_T-E52-01.md` and filed on its recommendation). Mechanism, from the coordinator's own two occurrences: a `tw_update_state` call whose SECOND argument block was mis-tagged had that block's entire literal text — tag markup included — appended onto the tail of the FIRST argument, so `scope_decision_why` was persisted carrying a literal `<parameter name="pending_notes">[…]` fragment, and on the retry a `</scope_decision_why></invoke>` fragment. The server accepted both: the field is free text with a 2000-char zod ceiling and no shape check, so the only symptom was an `⛔ scope_decision_why: Too big` rejection once the swallowed text pushed the total over the ceiling — a length error standing in for a structural one, which is exactly the kind of misleading signal that costs retries (it cost three here, and the first successful write persisted the contamination). Why it is worth a row rather than a shrug: `scope_decision_why` and `pending_notes` are the audit trail for cut approval and escalation, the E17 record-integrity rule exists because this class of record is load-bearing, and contamination is invisible to every existing gate — `tw_get_state` renders the junk back verbatim, and a later reader cannot tell a tag fragment written by a broken tool call from one deliberately quoted in prose. Fix options: **(i)** reject at the tool boundary — a free-text handoff field whose value contains a `<parameter name=`/`</` style fragment is almost certainly a malformed call, so fail it loudly with a dedicated code instead of letting a length ceiling report it; **(ii)** advisory only — surface a `warnings` entry (the E28/E33 shrink-warning precedent) and let the write land; **(iii)** content-only — an E17-adjacent line telling every role to re-read a long attestation field back after writing it. Note the honest limit of (i): the same fragments are legitimate content when a row quotes them, which is precisely what this row does — so the predicate must be narrow (fragment at the very tail, unbalanced) or it will red on truthful prose, the E74 false-positive lesson. Measure first: two occurrences, both from one session and one root cause (a client-side malformation, not a server bug), so option (iii) may well be the whole honest answer. **SECOND OCCURRENCE, 2026-08-20/21, during E43's own chain — and it is materially worse than the filed one, so the P3 label is now questionable (recorded by pm intake at the E43 release handback, not re-derived): the class reached an EVIDENCE ARTIFACT for the first time.** The qa-engineer PASS write for `e43-test-file-ask-at-dispatch` had its second argument block mis-tagged; the block's entire literal text (a stray closing tag plus a literal opening parameter tag for the next argument) was absorbed onto the tail of `qa_review`, and `pending_notes` landed EMPTY. Because the server auto-records `qa_review` as PASS evidence per completed id, the polluted text was written verbatim into BOTH `qa_reports/review_T-E43-01.md` and `qa_reports/review_T-E43-02.md` — i.e. the corruption propagated from a handoff field into the on-disk artifacts the evidence gates and every later reader trust. The filed instance only ever polluted `scope_decision_why`. **Two new facts the filed row does not capture.** (a) The pollution is SILENT AND DOUBLE: no gate fires, and the auto-record fan-out multiplies one malformed call into N artifacts. (b) **The repair path is blocked by design**: the write that would have restored the lost `pending_notes` was correctly rejected `TRANSITION_REJECTED` — there is no `qa-engineer:PASS → qa-engineer:PASS` edge, PASS being terminal — so a same-tuple correction is impossible and the only alternatives are fabricating a transition off PASS (rejected as worse than the loss) or leaving the record incomplete. The notes were re-homed into `qa_reports/archive/e43-test-file-ask-at-dispatch/review_T-E43-02.md` instead, which is **exactly the E55 mechanism** (findings reachable only if the next reader remembers to look) arriving by a second route. This raises (i)/(ii) — server-side arg validation rejecting a field whose value carries tool-call tag markup — above the (iii) prose option: prose cannot stop a malformed call, and the disclosure-and-clean remediation this occurrence needed took a human-visible detour through two artifacts. Cheap concrete shape for (i): reject any `tw_update_state` string arg matching a tag-markup signature, naming the field — the arg never legitimately contains one | P3 (severity under review — see the 2026-08-21 second occurrence) | — | ~1–2 (`tools/handoff-orchestrator.ts` arg validation for (i)/(ii), or a `content/` line for (iii)) | **DONE** (shipped v3.111.0) — a zod `superRefine` at the `tools/registry.ts` write boundary refuses leaked tool-call markup on five free-text fields (`pending_notes` entries, `scope_decision_why`, `qa_review`, `blocking_reason`, `tw_add_task` description). Predicate deliberately narrow — tail-anchored AND carrying a real tool-call signal (close-tag slash or attribute assignment) — after round 1 rejected bare `<placeholder>` tails including a verbatim coordinator SOP line and TS generics, and round 2 left a greedy unterminated-quote branch swallowing trailing prose. A predicate that reds on truthful prose is worse than no predicate |

(i) is nearly free and prevents the class; (iii) catches what (i) misses. Concrete instances: E39, E40 and E72 all read `—` in their `status` cell while *Recommended execution order* records them **DONE and shipped** (v3.99.0, v3.100.0, v3.103.0 respectively) — three rows, three shipped features, all still presenting as open in the table a reader hits first. Why it matters beyond tidiness: the Backlog Intake Loop (`coord-03`) instructs the coordinator to read the backlog "in its own declared order … skip done-marked rows", so a reader following the SOP against the ticket table re-picks shipped work, and a reader following the order table gets the right answer only because the order rows happen to be maintained. Two sources of truth for one fact, and the authoritative one is the SECOND one you find. Root cause is structural, not laziness: releases amend the order row (release-engineer's step 7c allowlist and every recorded close-out do exactly that) while the ticket row's status cell has no owner and no step that touches it. Fix options: **(i)** declare the order table the single source of truth and DELETE the ticket table's `status` column outright — the cheapest honest fix, and the one this file's own history supports since the order rows are what everyone actually reads; **(ii)** give the status cell an owner — add a step to release-engineer's close-out that marks it, accepting the write-scope growth; **(iii)** a `scripts/` check that fails when a ticket row reads `—` while its order row says DONE. All three DO carry a bold `DONE` inside their `desc` cell, so the ticket table is not in fact presenting them as open, and option **(i)** (delete the status column) is VOID — there is nothing to delete. What survives is a narrower but real class: **the done-mark is free text with no fixed position**. Measured 2026-08-21 across the live order table: 8 of its 20 done rows buried the token mid-cell or dropped the word DONE entirely (rows 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 8c, 8j, 10 — row 5/E57's mark sits at ~char 600 of its cell, row 8j's at ~char 500). **Consequence, observed the same session**: a reader who truncates a long cell reports shipped tickets as unmarked — E57 and E80 were both reported open before a full-cell re-read corrected it, i.e. this row's own failure mode reproduced by a different mechanism than the one filed. **Half executed 2026-08-21**: all 8 rows normalized to lead with `**DONE / PARTIAL / VOID — shipped vX.Y.Z**`, which is the cheapest part of option (iii). The remaining decision is only whether to pin that leading-token convention with a check (`scripts/`-side, qa-owned) or leave it as convention — and note that any such check must honor escaped `\|` inside code spans, the E74 trap, which a naive splitter fails on this file's own order row 8h. **RECURRED 2026-08-28 at the v3.105.0 close-out, and the E74 trap is what caused it**: release-engineer's step 7c wrote E96's done-mark into what it computed as "column 6" of that row — but the E96 row carried two UNESCAPED pipes inside a code span (`default \| no_nudges \| counter_steer`, escaped by the coordinator when fixing this), so the row was really 8 columns and the mark landed in the trailing cell instead of leading the desc cell. Net effect for a reader: the desc column still described an open problem while the design-link column held a paragraph. Both were corrected coordinator-direct the same day. This is the first occurrence AFTER the 2026-08-21 normalization, it was produced by a role following its SOP correctly rather than by carelessness, and it fires exactly where the row above predicted (a naive column splitter meeting an unescaped pipe) — which upgrades option (iii) from tidiness to a real guard, and says the check must validate BOTH the leading-token convention AND pipe escaping, since the second is what defeated the first. | P3 | — | ~1 for (i) (`docs/backlog.md` column removal), ~2 for (ii)/(iii) | **DONE** (shipped v3.111.0) — ADVISORY-ONLY done-mark position rule in `scripts/check-md-tables.mjs`, never affecting the exit code, scoped to `docs/backlog.md` by exact header-cell match. Fatal would have required normalizing rows in a file shared with seven concurrent Wave 1 lanes |

It also credits the `"Authoritative source."` fix in `specs/qa-flow-enforcement-architecture.md` to "E70 R2-3" when it is E62's N7. Both are the author writing a summary from the ticket text rather than from the diff — a plausible-sounding attribution nobody verifies, in a repo whose current backlog theme is prose asserting what the source does not. Zero runtime risk and the fix for any single instance is one sentence, which is exactly why it recurs: `verify-release`'s CHANGELOG check asserts the entry EXISTS for the version, never that its claims match `git diff --cached --name-only`. Worth noting this defect and E94 were produced by the same release and are near-inverses — E94 shipped a tree missing a file the CHANGELOG claimed, E95 named files the diff never touched — so a single check comparing the entry's cited paths against the staged diff would have caught both. Fix options: (i) SOP-only — step 10 must derive the entry's file citations from `git diff --cached --name-only`, never from the backlog row; (ii) a `scripts/` check that extracts backtick-quoted paths from the new CHANGELOG section and fails when one is absent from the release commit's file list (qa-owned test surface; note it must tolerate legitimately-cited paths that are context rather than changes — the E83 distinction, which is why that row is a dependency in spirit if not in order); (iii) accept and rely on review, which is what happened here and it took a post-release audit to catch. Prefer (i) first and treat (ii) as the durable form once E83's changed-vs-cited distinction exists to build on | P3 | E94, E83 | ~1 for (i), ~3 for (ii) | — |
| E96 | **DONE — shipped v3.105.0** (2026-08-28): Resolved as option (i), content-only, two coordinator fragments: `content/coord-02-host-dispatch.md` now states that a host prompt nudging the agent away from subagent calls is not a reason to take the Fallback — an explicit `/teamwork` invocation IS the user's request — and adds a WHEN/DO clause requiring a fallback taken while the host DOES advertise `Task` to be surfaced in chat as a §3.2 builder ≠ judge downgrade. `content/coord-03-core-fallback.md` re-conditions the fallback on genuine tool unavailability and retires the unqualified "graceful and silent" framing. Option (ii) deferred to **E99** (schema bump); **E100** filed from this feature's own review round. **The host system prompt biases the coordinator away from subagent dispatch, and the resulting downgrade is invisible to every gate** (found by the coordinator 2026-08-27 while answering a user question about its own system prompt). Claude Code's system prompt carries `"Do not call the AgentTool unless the user requested it"` — hardcoded in the CLI binary as system-prompt section `heron_brook`, resolved at assembly time from client data -> GrowthBook flag `tengu_heron_brook` -> a model-cohort fallback to the literal, with sibling steer arms `default \| no_nudges \| counter_steer` selectable via `CLAUDE_CODE_THISTLE_GREBE` / `tengu_silent_harbor`. Present in every CLI version still on disk (2.1.245/246/247, 2026-08-25..27); earlier binaries are auto-update-deleted, so the introduction date is not locally recoverable. **Not a local setting** — absent from every `settings.json`, `CLAUDE.md`, output-style, plugin, and shell wrapper on the machine, and it can activate or deactivate server-side with no local change. **Measured non-impact, so this is not an incident row:** 259/260 `Agent` dispatches across all projects returned OK (the one failure was a user interrupt, 2026-08-11), `dispatch_pins.sr-engineer="fable"` was honored on both 2026-08-24 chains, and v3.104.2/.3/.4 all shipped in-window. **The exposure is structural.** §3.2 builder != judge is context-topological: `Agent(subagent_type="code-reviewer")` yields a judge that never saw the builder's reasoning; `tw_switch_role("code-reviewer")` loads the same SOP into the context that just wrote the code. The two produce byte-identical handoff records, no entry in the 33-row `GATE_REGISTRY` validates writer context, and subagent transcripts are not persisted (`isSidechain` = 0 across 49,619 recorded transcript lines), so the downgrade is undetectable both live and forensically. `content/coord-03-core-fallback.md:1` names this exact behavior and calls it *"graceful and silent"* — silent is the defect: the fallback is correct for hosts that genuinely lack the Task tool, and wrong when taken under an external nudge on a host that has it. Fix options, cheapest first: (i) content-only — state in the Auto-Routing / fallback fragments that an explicit `/teamwork` invocation IS the user request for subagent dispatch, and that the fallback is reserved for genuine tool unavailability; ships to every managed workspace through normal prompt composition and needs no server change; (ii) (i) plus make the degradation loud — record on the handoff which mechanism carried each role hop, so a chain judged in-context is distinguishable after the fact from one judged in a clean context; (iii) a server-side gate rejecting a judge write from a non-independent context — **not implementable**, the server cannot observe client context topology; recorded so it is not re-proposed. Prefer (i)+(ii): (i) removes the bias, (ii) makes the residual honest. Same family as E91 — both are "dispatch integrity is asserted by the client and verified by nothing" | P1 (human priority call, 2026-08-27) | — (E91 in spirit) | ~3 (`content/coord-02-host-dispatch.md` + `content/coord-03-core-fallback.md` + test pin) | — |
| E97 | **DONE — shipped v3.104.5** (2026-08-27): extracted `isReleaseClosingWrite()` predicate into shared export from `gates/feature-lease.ts`, consumed by `tools/handoff-parse.ts:491` to skip advisory on release-closing write (`last_agent="release-engineer" ∧ next_role="pm"`). Observable behavior change: `tw_get_state` no longer emits stale-dispatch advisory on terminal closing writes. **The E1A terminal closing write makes every released feature report a permanent false `stale_dispatch` advisory** (found by the coordinator 2026-08-27 while ground-truthing E60's close-out; the advisory is what triggered that Crash-Resume pass, on a feature that had closed cleanly two days earlier). `tools/handoff-parse.ts:491` computes the advisory from `next_role && dispatched_at && elapsed > 15 min`, with **no exclusion for the terminal marker**. But release-engineer's step-12 closing write is *required* to set `next_role="pm"` (`content/skill-release-engineer.md:216` — the E1A exact triple `gates/feature-lease.ts` keys on), and every `tw_update_state` stamps `dispatched_at`. So 15 minutes after any successful release the state reports `stale in-flight dispatch: pm` for a dispatch that never occurred, and keeps reporting it — the stamp never refreshes — until a new `active_feature` starts. **Measured live:** E60 closed 2026-08-25T03:04:47Z with the correct triple (`last_agent="release-engineer"`, `status="In_Progress"`, `next_role="pm"`, `pending_notes[0]="Released v3.104.4"`) and `tw_get_state` on 2026-08-27 returned `elapsed_minutes: 3073` against a 15-minute threshold. Cost is not cosmetic: the advisory's own message instructs the reader to run the Crash-Resume Protocol, so every post-release session pays a full ground-truth pass (`git status`/`diff`/`log` plus re-reading every claimed file) to rediscover that nothing is wrong — and it trains the reader to discount a signal whose entire value is being rare. Root cause is a conflated pair: `next_role` is documented as *"advisory metadata"* routing while `dispatched_at` means "a subagent was actually launched"; the closing write sets the former as a **terminal** signal and the predicate reads the pair as a dispatch. Fix options, cheapest first: (i) exclude the terminal marker in the predicate — skip when `last_agent === "release-engineer" && status === "In_Progress" && next_role === "pm"`, the same tuple `gates/feature-lease.ts` already recognizes, so the two agree on what "closed" means; (ii) add the `pending_notes[0]` `/^Released v/` disjunct as well, matching E13's terminal-marker resilience fallback, so a closing write whose transient `next_role` was dropped by a heal-write is still excluded; (iii) stop stamping `dispatched_at` on the closing write, separating "routing hint" from "subagent launched" at the source — cleanest semantically but it touches the write path and every consumer of the field. Prefer (i)+(ii): read-path-only, they mirror an existing predicate rather than inventing one, and (ii) covers the two incident classes E13 already documented | P2 | E13, E22 | ~2 (`tools/handoff-parse.ts` + qa-owned pins in the D5 stale-dispatch test) | — |

':!.current'` and `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard -- . ':!.current'`. The leading `.` pathspec scopes both to the current working directory, so an operator running step 8 from any subdirectory gets empty output and a **silent PASS** — the exact failure the check exists to prevent, in the exact shape (a quiet green where a loud STOP belongs) that let v3.104.2 ship. It cannot fire today only because step 8's earlier `git add -- lib/ tools/ ...` line fails loudly first from a subdirectory — a fragile accident of ordering, not a structural guarantee. Any future edit making that `git add` line subdirectory-tolerant (E71a's existence pre-filter is already halfway there) re-arms this silently. Fix: `':/'` and `':!:/.current'` — repo-root-relative magic pathspecs, unaffected by cwd; two tokens, no behavior change at the repo root. `test/release-staging.test.mjs`'s new AC2(E94) test already extracts the commands from the live SOP text and executes them across four scratch-repo shapes, so a fifth shape (invoked from a subdirectory) is the natural pin and needs no new harness. **N1, the companion half:** the same paragraph's STOP remedy says "stage them" unconditionally, with no branch for a stray untracked file that should not ship at all. It is recorded here as a companion to be taken by whichever ticket next opens that bullet, not as work of its own. Fix options: (i) N2 alone — the pathspec swap plus its fifth-shape pin; (ii) N2 with N1 folded in, rewording both STOP remedies together; (iii) accept — but note that accepting N2 means relying on a `git add` failure that is one edit away from disappearing. That strengthens option (i)/(ii) in one specific way worth designing to: a `dispatch_mechanism` field that records only task-vs-switch_role would NOT have surfaced this, because all three hops were genuine `Task` dispatches. If the field is built, it should carry the tier actually reported alongside the mechanism, so a pin-vs-actual divergence is countable rather than, as here, noticed by chance in a chat transcript that will not survive compaction. **SECOND INSTANCE THE SAME DAY, with a shipped consequence**: the v3.105.1 release-engineer reported `(haiku)` against its own `recommended_model: opus`, and the resulting release documented a deliverable that does not exist, named a non-existent path, and asserted a constitution property that is verifiably absent — corrected in v3.105.2 and filed as **E103**. Use it for role switching when context budget permits"* — a capability statement with a budget escape hatch, silent on judge roles and silent on the host nudge. **The nudge is real, hardcoded, and not client-removable**: Claude Code assembles ~27 named system-prompt sections at startup, one of which is `heron_brook`, whose model-cohort fallback is the literal `JGe = ["Do not call the AgentTool unless the user requested it", "Do not use workflows or deep-research unless the user requested it"]` (verified in `~/.local/share/claude/versions/2.1.251`). Resolution order is client-data `tengu_heron_brook` -> GrowthBook `tengu_heron_brook` -> that literal when `qWt(model)` holds, i.e. the model carries `opus_5_prompt_bundle` AND GrowthBook `tengu_fennel_godwit` is false. There is **no local lever**: `FENNEL_GODWIT` has zero env-form occurrences in the binary (contrast `tengu_thrifty_sonic`, which does expose `CLAUDE_CODE_THRIFTY_SONIC`), and the string is absent from every `settings.json`, `settings.local.json`, `CLAUDE.md`, output-style and plugin on the machine — so it cannot be deleted client-side and can be re-armed server-side with no local change. Note the CLI's own internal tension, recorded so it is not mistaken for an agc problem: the section immediately preceding `heron_brook` is `subagent_steer_delegation`, i.e. the delegation guidance the nudge then counter-steers. **Why this is NOT a duplicate of E100 and NOT a workaround for it** — the load-bearing distinction, and the reason the row exists: E96 v3.105.0 put the correct disambiguation into `content/coord-02-host-dispatch.md`, E100 records that it never renders (`prompts/skill-manifest.ts:70` tags it `host:claude-code`; `:87-88` gates that on `host === "claude-code"`; neither this repo's nor the adopter acceptance project's `.current/.config.json` carries a `host` key), and fixing E100 makes it render — **but coord-02 is agc telling itself through an MCP-injected prompt.** The nudge's condition clause is `unless the user requested it`, and `CLAUDE.md` is the channel Claude Code itself loads *as user instruction*. So this row is the only channel that literally satisfies the clause rather than arguing around it, and it renders regardless of the `host` key — it is worth shipping if E100 never ships, and stays worth having after E100 does. **Live instance, not projection**: the SEL19 chain ran `pm -> sr-engineer -> code-reviewer` with zero dispatches, recorded `review_verdict: CHANGES_REQUESTED` under conditions where §3.2 builder != judge was structurally impossible, and was caught only by the acting agent's voluntary bias disclosure ~20 minutes and three hops later; the next hop was `qa-engineer`, which owns `tw_complete_task` and would have authored its own PASS. That workspace has no `host` key, so coord-02 was dead text there — E96's remedy was present in the server and absent from the session. **Fix**: amend the `## Execution Profile — Claude Code` dispatch bullet in `templates/agent-adapters/claude.md` to state that invoking `/teamwork` (or any agc role prompt) IS the user's standing request for subagent dispatch, that a host prompt nudging away from it does not override that, and that `code-reviewer` / `qa-engineer` MUST be dispatched via `Task` because in-context judging voids §3.2 and is invisible to all 33 gates — build roles may still run in-context under budget pressure. **`claude.md` ONLY.** `codex.md` and `antigravity.md` are deliberately untouched: those hosts carry no `heron_brook`, and some advertise no Task tool at all, so asserting the obligation there is a false promise of a capability the host may not have. **Rejected alternative, recorded so it is not re-proposed**: putting the line in the user's global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`. Wrong axis — it fixes one machine's every project instead of every machine's agc projects, does not travel with `git clone` (so teammates and new laptops silently lose it), and injects judge-role governance into repos that have never heard of agc. The template is a tracked file and therefore already travels the axis the problem has. **Two scope facts the cut must carry**: (a) the block is marker-delimited (`bin/agc-init.mjs:24-25`) so `writeClaudeBlock` refreshes it in place and leaves the adopter's own text outside the markers untouched — but **existing workspaces do not auto-update**, `agc init` must be re-run per workspace, which is a release-note obligation, not an implementation detail; (b) E73 constraint (7) already records the adapter stamps as tracked agc artifacts some adopters will object to, and this lengthens that block — not a new class, but it should not be discovered as a surprise later. | E102 | **R3-A DECIDED 2026-09-16 BY HUMAN: symlinks ARE a supported layout; hardlinks explicitly are NOT.** So `atomicWriteFile` must resolve the target before deriving `tmpPath` (`fs.realpathSync`), and write through the link instead of replacing it. **The argument that settled it was an internal contradiction, found 2026-09-16**: E111 shipped a rule in this very file telling adopters to **symlink** `qa_reports/`, `review_reports/` and `specs/` back to primary, and `checkWorktreeEvidence` spends most of its length using `lstatSync` / `isSymbolicLink` / `realpathSync` to verify those links are correct — while `atomicWriteFile`, a few hundred lines away in the same file, destroys a symlink by replacing its inode. Teaching a convention and breaking it in one binary is not defensible, and 'neither this repo's nor the adopter acceptance project's `CLAUDE.md` is a link' proves only that WE do not use the layout, not that adopters do not — and E73's own framing is that this row class serves adopter workspaces rather than this one. Combined with a failure that is **silent** (no error, and `agc check` then reports OK against the detached copy, so the user believes it applied), a ~2-line fix is the obvious trade. **Two implementation points the cut must not miss.** (1) `realpathSync` THROWS on a dangling symlink — handle it fail-closed, and note the precedent already exists a few hundred lines away in `checkWorktreeEvidence`, which catches exactly this and treats an unresolvable link as not-outside. (2) **Hardlinks are out of scope by decision, not by oversight**: `realpathSync` cannot resolve a hardlink because a hardlink has no target — it is a second name for the same inode — so `renameSync` breaks it just as silently and this fix does not help. The only thing that would is reverting to a truncating in-place write, which is precisely what T-E100-03 replaced to remove the `^C`/ENOSPC truncation risk. That risk is likelier and worse than a broken hardlink, so hardlinks are declared unsupported and the cut should say so in the code comment rather than leaving the next reader to re-derive it. **Five non-blocking findings from E100+E101's round-3 review, all in `bin/agc-init.mjs`, one of them a behaviour regression that feature introduced** (filed 2026-08-31 from `review_reports/review_T-E100-01.md` Round 3, which APPROVED with zero must-fix and ranked all five file-a-row). **R3-A — a symlinked or hardlinked `CLAUDE.md` is now REPLACED rather than written through.** Demonstrated end-to-end, not inferred: the canonical target kept its old content and never received the E101 bullet, while the link became a detached regular file; `agc check` then reports OK against the detached copy, so the feature silently fails to apply exactly where the user believes it did. Introduced by T-E100-03's `atomicWriteFile` conversion (tmp + `renameSync` replaces the inode; the previous truncating write followed the link). Accepted as non-blocking because no data is destroyed and the delta still cuts net risk — truncation on `^C`/ENOSPC is likelier and worse — and qa has since pinned today's behaviour as a documented-behaviour test, so a future fix is deliberate rather than accidental. Fix is ~2 lines (`fs.realpathSync(target)` before deriving `tmpPath`), but **it needs a human call first**: are project-root `CLAUDE.md` symlinks (dotfiles-managed, monorepo-shared) a supported layout? Neither this repo's nor the adopter acceptance project's `CLAUDE.md` is a link, which is why it did not block the migration. **R3-B — the new reparse-guard comment names the wrong predicate.** `:224-225` says the value was "confirmed falsy" before the splice; the predicate actually confirmed is "not a non-empty string", and the two diverge on every truthy non-string (`42`, `{a:1}`, `["x"]`). A maintainer who believes "falsy" reads the guard at `:234` as a verbose `!reparsed.host` and simplifies it — which reopens the exact wrong-occurrence hole that guard exists to close, i.e. this one-word comment defect is the highest-leverage of the five relative to its size, and it is in-class with the very finding T-E100-03 (2) closed. **R3-C — `atomicWriteFile` has no `try/finally`**, so a failure after the tmp file is opened but before the rename (ENOSPC mid-write is the realistic case) strands a partial `.tmp` in the repo root; separately the write resets the target's mode, silently widening a `0600` `CLAUDE.md` to `0644`. **R3-D — the diagnostics channel contradicts its own comment**: `:288-292` justifies the separate stdout bucket on the grounds that "stderr is routinely discarded", and `:297-300` then puts the actionable reason on stderr. **R3-E — the new comments cite review-round identifiers that ship to adopters** (`:94` "T-E100-03 (1) — same class as the C3 fix below", `:330` likewise). This is **E73 constraint (4) firing inside this very cut**, which is the reason to fix it here rather than fold it into E73: that constraint's rule is "a comment MUST NOT cite anything its reader cannot reach", `C3` and `T-E100-03` are reachable only from this repo's own review reports, and E73 records that those trees are untracked in an adopter workspace. Batching note: R3-B through R3-E are comment/string edits plus one `try/finally`, i.e. a single small mini-chain; R3-A is the only one carrying a behaviour change and a human decision, so it can legitimately split off if the symlink answer is slow | P2 (proposed; R3-A needs a human call on supported layouts, R3-B is arguably worth pulling forward on leverage) | E100 ✓, E101 ✓ | ~2 (`bin/agc-init.mjs` + qa-owned pins extending `test/agc-adapters.test.mjs`, which already carries the R3-A documented-behaviour test) | **DONE** (shipped v3.111.0) — `bin/agc-init.mjs` resolves the target with `realpathSync` before deriving `tmpPath`, so a write follows a symlinked `CLAUDE.md` instead of detaching and replacing it. `atomicWriteFile`'s tmp + `renameSync` swapped the inode, so the canonical file the user maintains never received the adapter block and `agc check` then reported OK against the detached copy — silent failure exactly where the user is most confident it worked. Settled by an internal contradiction rather than a preference: E111 ships a rule in this same binary telling adopters to symlink evidence directories back to primary. Also adds try/finally against a stranded partial `.tmp` on ENOSPC and preserves the target's mode so a deliberate `0600` no longer widens to `0644`. Hardlinks unsupported by decision, now stated in code. **Diverges from Wave 0 decision E**, recorded not hidden: the fail-closed catch is unreachable because `existsSync` is false for a dangling link at every call site; `agc init` instead repairs the link. | E103 | **DECIDED 2026-09-16 — option (iii) — BUT THE DECISION TEXT WAS UNEXECUTABLE AND THE LANE CORRECTLY DEVIATED (2026-09-17).** `agc init` never scaffolds `content/`, so a coordinator running in an adopter workspace cannot read `content/skill-<role>.md` at all — the server resolves that directory from its own install root (`tools/role.ts:22`), not from the workspace. **The verification recorded below is the wrong check**: it compared the eleven `content/skill-*.md` values against `~/.claude/agents/*.md`, two sources that both exist only in this repo's environment, and never asked whether either exists in a managed workspace. Root cause and the standing pre-cut check are in `docs/v4.0.0-execution-plan.md` §8b. The original text, preserved: the coordinator's dispatch section reads `recommended_model` from the role's own SOP frontmatter and passes it as the `model` override on EVERY `Task` dispatch, with `dispatch_pins` overriding it when the human has set one. Chosen over (i) because (i) relies on the coordinator remembering, which is the same class of failure the row exists to prevent, and over (ii) because a role cannot reliably observe its own tier. **Cheap to build**: `tools/skill-frontmatter.ts` already parses `recommended_model`, so this is wiring, not new machinery. **Verified 2026-09-16 before deciding**: the eleven `content/skill-*.md` `recommended_model` values and the installed `~/.claude/agents/*.md` `model` values agree everywhere, so (iii) introduces no conflict with the subagent definitions — it makes explicit what the definitions already say, which is precisely what removes the silent-default failure. **Adopted as coordinator practice immediately**, ahead of the cut: every dispatch from 2026-09-16 carries an explicit `model`. Note the pin is not decorative — `sr-engineer` frontmatter reads `opus` while this workspace's standing human pin is `fable`, so `dispatch_pins` genuinely overrides on every sr dispatch. **A role dispatched without an explicit model override silently ran four tiers below its own `recommended_model`, and produced the exact record-integrity failure its SOP marks CRITICAL** (filed 2026-08-31 from the v3.105.1 release; every measurement below taken the same session). `content/skill-release-engineer.md` frontmatter declares `recommended_model: opus` and `templates/claude-code-agents/release-engineer.md:3` pins `model: opus`, but the v3.105.1 release-engineer reply watermarked `(haiku)` — the coordinator passed no dispatch-time override, so the subagent definition's default applied and nothing anywhere compared the two. **The consequences are measured, not projected, and all three are the E17 "describe the diff, not the brief" class that `content/skill-release-engineer.md:24` marks CRITICAL:** (a) the shipped CHANGELOG and the published GitHub release described E101 as *"adapter stamp references updated ... to reflect that the server version declaring `host` is 3.105.1"* — routine version bookkeeping — while E101 was actually the judge-dispatch obligation rewritten into `templates/agent-adapters/claude.md`, so the entry documented a non-deliverable and omitted the real one entirely; (b) it named `templates/agent-adapters/CLAUDE.md`, a path that does not exist (the file is lower-case `claude.md`), which is the specific check that SOP line requires (`git diff --stat` before writing, never from memory of the brief); (c) it asserted in Notes that *"Constitution §1 watermark and the roles' own SOPs already name the cost of `host` declaration"* — verified false: zero occurrences of `host` in `content/const-01-core-head.md` and no host-declaration language in any `const-*.md` or `skill-*.md`. Separately it collapsed execution-order rows `0d` and `0e` from 4 columns to 3 by overwriting the `intake` cell with the done-mark — the E74 escaped-pipe/column-split trap, **third recurrence**, and this one landed despite the dispatch brief naming E88's prior recurrence explicitly and telling it to verify column counts after writing. **Why this is its own row rather than an E99 sub-case**: E99 is about a hop's mechanism and tier being unrecorded and therefore unauditable *after the fact*; this row is about the pin never being *applied* in the first place, which is a dispatch-time defect with a different fix surface. Note the reinforcing detail: `skill-release-engineer.md:21` already cites a haiku dispatch as the origin of the E9A hand-edit forensics, so the role's own SOP documents low-tier dispatch as a known hazard and still has no mechanism that prevents it. Fix options: **(i)** coordinator pins `release-engineer` at dispatch — cheapest, but it relies on the coordinator remembering, which is the same class of failure it is meant to prevent; **(ii)** the role self-checks its own tier against its frontmatter and refuses or escalates on a downgrade — likely **unimplementable for the same reason E96 option (iii) was**, since a role cannot reliably observe its own tier (and E99's 2026-08-31 measurement shows the reported tier already disagrees with the pin); **(iii)** make the pin mechanical rather than remembered — the coordinator SOP's dispatch section reads `recommended_model` from the role's frontmatter and passes it as the `model` override on every `Task` dispatch, with `dispatch_pins` overriding it when the human set one. `tools/skill-frontmatter.ts` already parses `recommended_model`, so the data path exists and (iii) is mostly SOP text plus a coordinator-fragment edit. Prefer (iii); (i) is the stopgap until it ships. **SECOND FACET, measured the same day inside this row's OWN corrective patch — the coordinator's dispatch brief is itself an unguarded contamination source, and option (iii) does not touch it.** Writing the v3.105.2 brief, the coordinator asserted that the published v3.105.1 GitHub release notes "still carry defects 1-3 live" and made updating them the single most important step. That was inference presented as observation: the coordinator had run `gh release view v3.105.1 --json name,tagName`, never fetched `body`, and reasoned that the notes must derive from the CHANGELOG it HAD verified as wrong. The release-engineer probed the live body before acting and found all three defects confined to `CHANGELOG.md` (re-verified afterwards: the three defect strings occur 0 times in that release body), and separately observed that v3.105.1's COMMIT MESSAGE was already correct — which is itself the forensic evidence that only the CHANGELOG had been written from a brief instead of a diff. **The asymmetry is the finding.** `content/skill-release-engineer.md:24` binds the release role to "describe the diff, not the brief", and that rule demonstrably WORKED twice in one dispatch: the role caught the coordinator's false claim, then caught its own first-draft repetition of it before `gh release create`, leaving the bad claim only in an immutable commit message it correctly declined to force-push away. **No equivalent rule binds the coordinator**, whose factual claims enter every downstream brief unchallenged and are trusted precisely because it is the one role reading state, git and the tree directly — so its errors propagate with more authority than any other role's. Fix shape, reusing a rule the codebase already ships and has now been observed working: extend the `:24` obligation to coordinator dispatch briefs via a `content/coord-*.md` line — a factual claim about the tree, a diff, or a published artifact must be verified at brief-writing time, and any claim the coordinator has NOT verified must be labelled as an assumption for the receiving role to check rather than stated as fact. Note the self-referential warrant, recorded because it is the strongest argument the row has: this instance was produced while writing the corrective patch for this very row, by the role best positioned to know better, in the same message that quoted `:24` at the release-engineer | P1 (proposed — it has a shipped, corrected-in-v3.105.2 instance, which is a stronger warrant than E100 carried at filing) | E99 (sibling, not blocking) | ~2 (`content/coord-02-host-dispatch.md` dispatch section + a qa-owned pin; more if (ii) is attempted) | **DONE** (shipped v3.111.0) — option (iii): every Task dispatch now carries an explicit `model`, resolved from the role's `dispatch_pins` entry else its `~/.claude/agents/<role>.md` `model` frontmatter, with no resolvable tier a stop-and-report rather than a silent omission. The bundle previously described the tier as something that happened to a dispatch and never told the coordinator to pass one — the measured consequence was a release-engineer running four tiers below its declared model and shipping three record-integrity defects. **Amends Wave 0 decision C**, deliberately visibly: decision C named `content/skill-<role>.md` frontmatter as the source, but `agc init` never scaffolds `content/` into a managed workspace, so read literally it resolves to nothing for every adopter. Intent preserved, artifact changed |
