| E106 | **DONE — merged to main 2026-09-28 (lane e106, `integ/e106`)**: `agc init --artifacts=local\|repo` (non-interactive; default `local` only when no artifact path is already tracked — otherwise the key stays undeclared and the tracked list is printed, human ruling at cut); `local` writes root-anchored rules to `.git/info/exclude`; tracked paths are reported with the `git rm -r --cached` command, never run; choice persisted as `.current/.config.json` `artifacts` (config schema 1 → 2, stamp-only, absence = undeclared); `agc check` reports intent-vs-state drift as advisories (exit code unchanged); evidence: `review_reports/archive/e106-init-artifacts-flag/review_T-E106-01.md` (code review, round 1 approved), `qa_reports/archive/e106-init-artifacts-flag/review_T-E106-06.md` (qa, round 1 pass). Original row: **`agc init` never asks whether governance artifacts belong in the repo, so the adopter's first commit decides it by accident** (filed 2026-09-14 from the H8 discussion in `docs/agc-feedback-2026-09-08.md`; carries E73's `(a)` untrack half, which that ticket retains only as a sequencing record). `bin/agc-init.mjs` is zero-flag and fully non-interactive (`init` / `check` only) and writes no ignore rule of any kind, so `.current/`, `tasks.md` and every runtime artifact land tracked in a shared repo by default — the exact path that produced the adopter acceptance project's mid-project untracking and its ~2541 now-dangling comment citations. **Four decisions are already taken (human, 2026-09-14) — this row is a cut, not a design question.** **(1) flag, not an interactive prompt**: `agc init --artifacts=local\|repo`; `agc init` is routinely run by an agent inside a session (`npx github:...`), where a readline prompt hangs. **(2) default `local`**: the irreversibility is asymmetric — local→repo is one `git add`, repo→local leaves the blobs in history (E104 precedent). **(3) `local` writes `.git/info/exclude`, not `.gitignore`**: zero footprint in a repo whose other contributors do not run agc; accepted cost is that it does not travel with a clone. **(4) already-tracked paths: detect and print, never run** — an exclude rule has NO effect on tracked files, so a user who runs `--artifacts=local` on a live repo is silently unprotected; the report must name the `git rm -r --cached ...` line AND state that history still contains the files. `git rm --cached` is not on the §6 sanctioned list and that commit reads as a deletion to teammates, so agc does not run it. Persist the choice as `.current/.config.json` `artifacts?: "local"\|"repo"` (schema 1 → 2, stamp-only / seeds-nothing per `docs/schema-versions.md`, **absence === undeclared, NOT `local`**) — the exclude file records STATE, the key records INTENT, and `agc check` needs both to tell "deliberately chose repo" from "never ran the new init". | P1 | — | ~3 (`bin/agc-init.mjs` + a `.config.json` migration + qa-owned assertions in `test/agc-adapters.test.mjs`; E100/E101 are the same shape) | — |
| E107 | **DONE — shipped as part of E231 (merge `f95e4ae`, 2026-09-28)**; **FOLDED into E231 (human-approved 2026-09-28) — E231's spec (`specs/e231-info-hygiene-rule.md`) ships this ticket's readability rule as part of one constitution-level bullet pair rather than a separate skill-file statement; this row closes as superseded once E231 ships. See that spec's "Relationship to E107" and "Decisions (human, 2026-09-28)" sections for what's carried over as-is and what's widened. The this-repo ticket-id exemption noted below is revoked as of 2026-09-28 — this repo is going public and its readers will no longer all run this tooling.** **Every human-readable thing agc produces assumes the reader runs agc, and nothing says otherwise** (filed 2026-09-14 from H6 + H7 + H9 in `docs/agc-feedback-2026-09-08.md`; filed as ONE ticket because they are one rule in three settings — splitting them would restate the same principle in three SOPs, which is what A8 and the constitution's "skills MUST NOT restate" exist to prevent). The constitution says nothing about comment content at all — `const-03`'s "don't touch adjacent comments" is the only comment rule in the corpus — so there is no rule against citing a ticket id, an AC number, or a gitignored path from code. Measured in the adopter acceptance project's `app/web/src` (non-test): 177 spec/report path citations, 293 ticket ids, 2071 AC references across 116 files, and a 33% comment-to-code ratio (worst files 66-69%), the bulk of it spec rationale transcribed into comments. **The rule to ship (wording settled 2026-09-14):** *anything written for a human assumes a reader who does not use agc* — no governance vocabulary (round / gate / `tw_*` / the PASS-FAIL protocol), no ticket ids, no citations of untracked paths; **machine fields, filename conventions and status values are explicitly OUT of scope** — they are protocol the server validates, and a rule that says "all artifacts must be human-legible" invites exactly the `pending_notes` bloat that E92 (silent field truncation) and E86 (field absorbing malformed-call text) already describe. The discriminator is prose-vs-protocol, not file-by-file, so it adapts per repo the same way "cite only tracked paths" does: in THIS repo `specs/` is tracked, so citing it is legal and the ~200 existing ticket-id comments stay (**this repo is exempt from the ticket-id half — its readers all run agc and `docs/backlog.md` is tracked, making `E26` a working index, not noise**); in a team repo the same sentence yields "cite `design/` only". Payoff beyond legibility: a generically-written `specs/` can be tracked, which lets comments cite it, which finally gives the "why" a home in the repo instead of forcing it into commit messages — and it is the precondition that makes E108 able to PRESERVE `specs/` on eject instead of deleting it. Investment is deliberately uneven: aim the effort at `design/` + `specs/`; `qa_reports/` prose stays as-is (nobody outside agc reads round-2 verdicts) and gets no rule and no check. | P1 | — | ~4 (one shared statement in `const-*.md` or a `content/partial-*.md`, referenced from `skill-pm.md` / `skill-design-auditor.md` / the sr-engineer surface, plus golden/render refresh) | — |
| E108 | **DONE — merged to main 2026-09-28 (lane e108, `integ/e108`)**: `agc eject` — dry-run by default prints the plan and the declared artifacts mode; `--yes` applies it; `--purge-knowledge` additionally removes domain knowledge; each path's disposition follows its actual git-tracked state (untracked paths are deleted, tracked ones are never deleted by agc — one consolidated `git rm -r` line is printed with a note that history keeps them), except agc's own host-trace files, which are edited or deleted in the working tree and reported as uncommitted changes; refuses inside a linked worktree and refuses `--yes` while lanes exist; states plainly what it cannot do (history, code comments, host MCP config, machine-wide subagent templates — commands printed instead). Human rulings 2026-09-28: `docs/backlog.md` is domain knowledge (kept by default, removed only under `--purge-knowledge`); machine-wide subagent templates are print-only; evidence: `review_reports/archive/e108-agc-eject/review_T-E108-01.md` (code review, rounds 1–2 approved), `qa_reports/archive/e108-agc-eject/review_T-E108-01.md` (qa, round 1 fail then round 2 pass), `qa_reports/archive/e108-agc-eject/review_T-E108-02.md` (tests, pass). Original row: **A project can adopt agc but never leave it: there is no `eject`, `remove` or `uninstall`, and no definition of what leaving would even mean** (filed 2026-09-14 from H4(b) in `docs/agc-feedback-2026-09-08.md`; H4(a), the per-feature teardown, stays with E73). Eject cannot be modelled as "undo init": `agc init` creates only four things (`.current/.config.json`, `tasks.md`, the marker-delimited `CLAUDE.md` adapter block, `AGENTS.md` / `.antigravityrules`) while everything else is runtime growth (`handoff.md`, `telemetry.jsonl`, `metrics.jsonl`, `specs/`, `qa_reports/`, `review_reports/`), so the removal set must be enumerated on its own. **Four classes, and the split is the whole design:** (i) machine state (`.current/*`) — remove; (ii-a) **domain knowledge (`design/`, `specs/`) — KEEP**; (ii-b) process evidence (`qa_reports/`, `review_reports/`, `tasks.md`, `docs/backlog.md`) — remove; (iii) host traces (adapter block, the `.git/info/exclude` rules) — remove. (ii-a) is kept because E107 makes those files generically readable, at which point they are no longer agc artifacts but project documentation that agc happened to produce — deleting them re-creates the rationale loss E107 exists to close. **Shape:** `agc eject` is dry-run and prints the plan; `agc eject --yes` executes the default disposition above; `agc eject --purge-knowledge` additionally deletes (ii-a) and is never the default. Dry-run-by-default is load-bearing rather than ceremonial — under E106's `local` default the artifacts are NOT in version control, so a mistaken delete has no `git checkout` to recover it, and this is agc's first destructive subcommand (init/check are additive-only). No interactive confirmation, for E106 decision (1)'s reason. **Three things eject must refuse to do and must say so:** it does not rewrite git history (artifacts that were ever tracked survive there — E104); it does not touch code comments (E107's citations are not machine-decidable); it does not edit the user's `.mcp.json` / `settings.json` host registration (print the command). Honest reporting of what it CANNOT clear is part of the deliverable, not a caveat. | P1 | E107 | ~3 (new subcommand in `bin/agc-init.mjs` + qa-owned cases in `test/agc-adapters.test.mjs`) | — |

| E110 | **DONE — shipped v3.117.0** (2026-09-24): `content/skill-pm.md` gains the Parallel-Lane Cut rule and a `touches` column in the cut table; `content/coord-01-core-head.md` Split Table gains the same column. Original row: **PM has no template or obligation for cutting work into parallel low-dependency lanes, although the pattern already ran a full round in production** (filed 2026-09-14 from H2 in `docs/agc-feedback-2026-09-08.md`; human's original ask: *"PM 這個角色在切票時, 要切成可以把任務平行開發且盡可能不互相依賴的狀態"*). An adopter acceptance project voided an 8-row sequential cut and re-cut SRCL into `S0 → L1..Ln (parallel) → J1a → J1b` on a single integration branch and one PR; git confirms the round completed (three lanes at `0 ahead`, all merged back). The hard invariant the human set: **L1-L4 never touch each other's files**, each with its own worktree / branch / `.current/` / session. **The step agc's SOP is missing is the lane-assignment rule, not the topology**: lane membership follows where an AC's `proof:` lands, NOT the AC's subject. Any `proof:` asserting on `SourceListView` / `NoNetworkBlock` DOM belongs to L2 regardless of topic, because that view is pure and prop-driven — its tests hand-build props, so L2 waits only on the `SourceListViewProps` type S0 froze, never on L1. Real parallelism comes from *prop-driven pure components + an early-frozen props type*, not from `depends_on` ordering. NDI's own draft mis-assigned AC8/AC12/AC13 to L1 and a per-AC `proof:` audit corrected them to L2 — **that audit is the missing PM step**. Ship as a template, not a general DAG: the shape is always the fixed three-stage seed → fan-out → join. Also formalize the `touches` (actual path set) column NDI hand-built into the cut table header at `content/skill-pm.md:113` and into `feature-split.md`. **Guardrail that must ship with it:** never parallelize for its own sake — PM keeps the right to declare `serial — shared layer`, and a shared seed must be serial-first (NDI's S0 freezes types only, no behaviour, which is the correct implementation of this guardrail). Open (待決 #3): `content/skill-pm.md` vs the coordinator Feature-Scope Gate; coordinator position is `skill-pm.md`, because the per-AC `proof:` audit is PM's action. | P1 | E109 | ~3 (`content/skill-pm.md` cut-table header + the template + golden refresh) | — |

| E112 | **DONE — shipped v3.115.0** (2026-09-22, v4 Wave 3): `tw_detect_drift` no longer lies in either direction — `tools/drift.ts`, `tools/evidence-lookup.ts`. Original row: **SECOND CASE ADDED 2026-09-17, MEASURED DURING WAVE 1** (Session B, L-RELTOOL/L-MDTOOL; folded into this row rather than filed separately because it is the same tool, the same code path and the same wave — doing it twice costs more than doing it once). **`tw_detect_drift` also lies in the single-workspace, multi-feature case.** After the lane's second batch began, the detector reported the FIRST batch's three already-PASSed tickets as vibe drift. Mechanism, verified by the coordinator: `completed_tasks` is feature-scoped and legitimately resets on an `active_feature` change, while `tasks.md` keeps the `[x]` marks from the previous feature — so the comparison at `tools/drift.ts:291` runs an empty ledger against a marked task list and every prior ticket looks like an unrecorded completion. The one mechanism that would absorb it, `driftBaselineIds` at `:280`, is maintained **only by release-engineer at SOP step 7b** (`content/skill-release-engineer.md:33` restricts the write to that role) — so the baseline advances once per RELEASE while `active_feature` changes many times per release. **Why it is this row's problem**: E112's existing case is drift being blind ACROSS workspaces; this is drift being wrong ACROSS features WITHIN one. Same detector, same fix surface, and a cut that closes one while leaving the other would be re-opening this file in a month. **The danger is not the noise, it is the remedy**: the natural response to reported vibe drift is `tw_sync`, which here would carry the previous feature's completion marks into the new feature's ledger. Session B recognised that and blocked it downstream by marking the ids known-drift in every brief — correct, but it is a manual guard on a detector that should not be lying. **Bites hardest right now**: multi-lane Wave sessions run two or three features per worktree by design. **`tw_detect_drift` reports clean while 6 of 7 tickets are unrecorded — the one tool built to catch this desync is structurally blind to the fan-out shape** (filed 2026-09-14 from H2-b B2 in `docs/agc-feedback-2026-09-08.md`). Primary's `handoff.completed_tasks` was `[SRCL-S0]` and primary's `tasks.md` had only `SRCL-S0` checked; the two agree, so drift is clean — while the true state was 7/7 PASS, the other six recorded in other workspaces' ledgers. It is not a miscount: the comparison is correct for a scope that is no longer the feature. Documented side effect: primary's `tw_get_next_task` still emits the voided `SRCL-ARCH01` (`[ ]`, `depends_on: none`), which is why NDI had to hand-append an `SRCL-VOID` marker row (see E117). No spec file, by design: backlog-row-as-spec, the mini-chain convention. |
| E113 | **DONE — shipped v3.115.0** (2026-09-22, v4 Wave 3): feature-level cost roll-up at feature close — `tools/feature-rollup.ts`, `scripts/feature-rollup.mjs`, `content/coord-03-core-fallback.md`. Original row: **Every feature-scoped cost brake divides by the number of lanes, so the `hop` cap overshot 2.8x in silence** (filed 2026-09-14 from H2-b B3 in `docs/agc-feedback-2026-09-08.md`). Measured on one NDI feature: the `hop` cap is 10, actual consumption was **8+4+6+4+6 = 28** (J1a's is unrecoverable — see E116 — so the true figure is higher), and any single read shows at most 8, so the cap **cannot fire**. `review_round` / `qa_round` become per-lane: four lanes can burn 12 CHANGES_REQUESTED rounds without ever locking to pm. Telemetry splits five ways (67/1/2/1/2), so `tw_gate_stats` — the E26 data source the E6 retirement retro runs on — is wrong for any fanned-out feature. The token brake would divide the same way: five `usage.jsonl` sidecars at ~20% each, so an 80% brake never trips (not yet bitten only because NDI sets no `tokenBudgetPerFeature`). Per E109 the fix is NOT to make the caps span lanes — per-lane budgets are correct for a lane. The recommendation is a **feature-level roll-up as an explicit PM/coordinator obligation at feature close**: list every lane's ticket, status and hop, and sum them, so a 28-vs-10 appears in front of a human at least once. Today only prose in a tracked backlog does this, by accident. **Data source settled 2026-09-16: E132** — the derived lane list (`git worktree list` plus each worktree's own handoff) is where the per-lane ticket/status/hop figures come from; this row defines the obligation to roll them up, E132 produces the facts. Reads sibling worktrees deliberately, unlike E115. |

The U+2028/U+2029 row-ERASURE vector measured in round 2 was deliberately not absorbed under the round cap and is filed as E131 (order `13s`). **Every `tasks.md` mutator passes caller text as a `String.replace` replacement string, so `$` expansion patterns in a note, reason or description splice real task rows into the file and report success** (found by code-reviewer during E117 rounds 1-3, `review_reports/review_T-E117-01.md` C5/C6, 2026-09-15; **file this WIDE — the round-2 formulation covered only the newline half and the reviewer is explicit that filing it narrow loses the rest**). `String.replace`'s replacement grammar interprets `$&`, `` $` ``, `$'`, `$1` and `$$` in the replacement string. `$&` and `$1` stay within the edited line; **`` $` `` (everything before the match) and `$'` (everything after) splice multi-line content**, and the reviewer reproduced both duplicating genuine task rows into `tasks.md` with `success: true` — one case moved `progress.total` from 3 to 4. Four sites share the hole: `tools/tasks-file.ts` `voidTaskInFile`, `rollbackTaskInFile`, `completeTaskInFile`, and `addTaskInFile`. E117 closed the consequence only for void, and only because there the injection defeats void's own operation (the row stays live while the tool reports success); for the other three the intended state change still lands **and — premise CORRECTED 2026-09-15, this row originally read "the injected row is inert garbage" and that is FALSE** (code-reviewer round 1 measured it: under the newline vector the spliced row is live and parseable, `getNextTask` offers it, and `progress.total` moves 2 → 3 on a `success: true` receipt) — which is why the reviewer declined to widen its own in-scope criterion in the round before the cap. The fix is one line per site — pass a replacer **function** instead of a replacement string, so no `$` sequence is ever interpreted. Not exploitable by an outside party: every caller is a governed role. The realistic trigger is a reason or note that quotes a task row verbatim, which is ordinary during a re-cut | P1 | E117 ✓ | ~2 (`tools/tasks-file.ts` 4 call sites + qa-owned cases) | — |

Original row: **ABANDONED-LANE DISPOSAL DECIDED 2026-09-16 (this row's own open question, settled so the cut is no longer blocked).** A lane that is voided, superseded or simply dropped never reaches release, so the writeback never runs and both of its outputs die with the worktree. **The two outputs are disposed differently because they are not the same kind of thing**: a **finding filed while working the lane stays true whether or not the lane ships**, so pending tickets are APPLIED (and get their ids allocated at that moment, exactly as on the shipped path); **evidence reports describe code that never shipped**, so they go to an `abandoned/` subtree or are discarded — the point is that it is an explicit decision rather than a silent loss. **Mechanism: `agc feature finish` is the ONLY sanctioned teardown**, with two paths — `--shipped` (the normal writeback) and `--abandoned` (apply findings, dispose of evidence, mark the lane abandoned in the record). That is E73's `finish` doing one more job, not a new mechanism, so it costs a branch rather than a component. A raw `git worktree remove` is thereby **undefined behaviour** and the docs must say so. **A detector is REQUIRED, not optional, and this is the part that decides whether the rule works.** Any scheme that relies on someone remembering to run a close-out will fail at exactly the moment it is needed, because abandoning a ticket is precisely when people stop paying attention to it. That is measured, not assumed: an adopter acceptance project lost, on 2026-09-08, the evidence for 4 of 7 tickets, and the cause was never that the harvest was done wrong — it was that the harvest step did not happen. E111 exists to delete that forgettable step; re-introducing one here would repeat the defect this repo just spent a release fixing. The detectable shape is cheap: **a branch carrying an unapplied pending-tickets file whose worktree no longer exists** is an orphan, and E132's derived lane list already supplies the live half of that comparison. Surface it from `agc check` or the Backlog Intake Loop. **A lane that files a new ticket mid-run allocates its id from a snapshot that cannot see other in-flight lanes, so two lanes can mint the same id and the collision survives the merge** (identified 2026-09-15 in the same design discussion). Two lanes forked at one base both read *max is E118* and both pick E119; both merge; because each appended its own row the collision is **semantically** invisible to a textual merge. Not theoretical — this repo files tickets mid-run routinely: E106-E118 came from one session, E119-E122 from another, and these very rows from a third. `git worktree list` is the correct cross-lane registry for *is someone already working this ticket* (it is authoritative, needs no naming convention, and surfaced a third worktree during this session that the filesystem scan of `.current/` could not see) but it cannot help here, because filing a ticket creates no worktree. **Fix: lanes never allocate.** A lane writes new tickets into a lane-local pending file with no id, and the release writeback assigns real ids at apply time, where exactly one actor writes and each apply observes the previous one's result — making collision structurally impossible rather than merely unlikely. **Open question the cut must settle rather than defer to sr-engineer**: what happens to a lane's pending tickets when the lane is abandoned rather than released (voided or superseded) — discard, force-apply, or require a release-engineer pass. `tw_void_task` (E117) is adjacent | P1 | E123 | ~2 (pending-file convention, release-engineer close-out) | — |

At PR merge, a lane's `tasks.md` rows and its pending backlog tickets should be written back to the shared copies with a summary plus a durable pointer, and the outer `tasks.md` and `docs/backlog.md` formally redeclared as **history indexes rather than live ledgers** — the `tw_*` tools then only ever read the lane-local copies, which is already true at runtime. This also answers the unbounded-growth complaint that motivated the discussion: the index carries a summary, and full granularity stays retrievable through the pointer. **The pointer must be the PR number, never a commit sha**: squash-merge rewrites a lane's shas, and this repo has its own pending history rewrite (E104 phase 1, prepared on a mirror, not yet pushed) that would invalidate every recorded sha. **Half the mechanism already exists** — release-engineer SOP step 7a already archives released evidence into `qa_reports/archive/<feature>/` and a parallel `review_reports/` subtree, and E111 shipped the constraint that only a top-level symlink carries that subtree — so extend 7a rather than invent a second archival path. **Evidence directories must NOT move into a per-lane directory**: they are append-only with globally unique filenames, they never conflict, and 7a already archives them; moving them would reintroduce the harvest-at-teardown step that E111 exists to delete **E125 CUT INPUTS FILED 2026-09-23 (Wave 4 intake, `NEW-TICKETS.md` L-SCHEMA section) — the cut must settle all three:** **(a) L-SCHEMA-NEW-1 — compaction, not just placement.** Measured: `tasks.md` 1004 lines / 483KB with ~99% closed rows; `docs/backlog.md` 659KB with a 21,876-char single row. Every session pays this in context, so the cut must decide HOW the outer files compact into an index (summary row + pointer; where closed rows go), not only where writeback lands. **(b) L-SCHEMA-NEW-2 — per-lane `tasks.md` and `specs/<feature>.md` move into `.current/<lane>/`** (human direction). They travel into `.current/history/<YYYY-MM>/<lane>/` at lane close under E123's package A′; `docs/backlog.md` stays global. The enabler is E123a's single `LANE_FILES` registry: one entry plus a schema migration step. **(c) L-SCHEMA-NEW-3 — the PR-number-only pointer fails the §8b adopter check.** PRs do not exist in every adopter workspace, and the server does not touch PR workflow. Direction: `pr` optional; always record `branch` + `base_sha` (the merge-base, stable under every merge strategy) + the ticket id; a ticket-id-in-commit-message convention makes `git log --grep` the universal fallback (a `content/` change, after E110). This amends the PR-number rule above and the Wave 6 card's matching prohibition. | E131 | **U+2028 / U+2029 in a task note or reason makes the row unparseable, so it stays on disk while every reader goes blind to it** (measured by code-reviewer as R2-C2 during the E121 round-2 review, 2026-09-15, and deliberately kept out of that capped chain rather than absorbed into it). `DEFAULT_TASK_REGEX`'s trailing `(.+)$` cannot cross **any** JavaScript line terminator, and JS counts U+2028 LINE SEPARATOR and U+2029 PARAGRAPH SEPARATOR as terminators even though `String.prototype.split("\n")` does not. So a note or reason carrying one leaves a physically single line that the regex refuses: the row survives in `tasks.md` but disappears from `parseTasksFromFile`, `getNextTask`, `tw_detect_drift` and `tw_sync` simultaneously. **Distinct from E121 and NOT closed by it**: E121's defect was row *forging* (a payload planting an extra parseable row), and for forging LF genuinely is the whole class — measured across LF/CRLF/CR/U+2028/U+2029/NEL/VT/FF/NBSP at all four mutators. This is row *erasure*, the opposite direction, and the reviewer measured it **identical at base and at the E121 working tree**, so it is pre-existing rather than a regression E121 introduced. Why it is worse than it sounds: the failure is silent in the one place the system is supposed to notice silence — `tw_detect_drift` compares the handoff ledger against what it can parse from `tasks.md`, so a row erased this way looks to the detector exactly like a row that was never there, the same symmetric-blindness family as E112, E120 and E126. Cheapest closure, already identified by the reviewer: widen E121's existing `containsLineBreak` helper from `/[\r\n]/` to `/[\r\n  ]/` — the guard, its four call sites and its refusal contract all already exist, so this is a one-character-class edit plus the qa cases that pin it. Consider in the same cut whether the *parser* should also be hardened rather than relying only on the input boundary, since a `tasks.md` hand-edited by a human never passes through the mutators at all | P2 | E121 ✓ | ~1 (`tools/tasks-file.ts` one regex) + qa-owned cases in the existing `test/e121-tasks-file-injection.test.mjs` | **DONE** (shipped v3.111.0) — U+2028/U+2029 can no longer erase a `tasks.md` row. Those terminators made a row unparseable, so it survived on disk while `parseTasksFromFile`, `getNextTask`, `tw_detect_drift` and `tw_sync` all went blind to it at once — not forged, rendered invisible. Scoped wider than filed: the input-boundary guard refuses both characters at all eight call sites, and the parser now fails loudly on a corrupted live row instead of silently erasing it, because a hand-edited `tasks.md` never passes a mutator at all |
| E132 | **No session can see what any other session is working on, and the one place that knows is never read** (identified 2026-09-16 while settling the worktree-default trigger; the gap was demonstrated live on 2026-09-15 — a coordinator working in the `e111` worktree had no idea a third worktree `agent-governance-mcp-e117` existed until it ran `git worktree list`, and nothing in `.current/` could have told it). Each worktree holds its own `.current/`, which is a different filesystem tree, so an in-flight lane is **structurally invisible** to its siblings: scanning `.current/` finds only lanes that already merged, which is exactly backwards from what is needed. **The fix is derivation, not registration.** `git worktree list --porcelain` already knows every worktree of the repo and its branch — the metadata lives in the shared `$GIT_COMMON_DIR`, so any lane can read it — and each worktree's own handoff supplies `active_feature`, `status`, `hop_count` and the current role. | E133 | **Two sessions can work the same ticket in the same workspace and nothing anywhere notices** (identified 2026-09-16 while settling the worktree-default trigger; split out of E132, which covers the cross-*worktree* half and explicitly defers this one). Three mechanisms look like they should catch it and none does: the **feature lease** keys on `active_feature`, so two sessions on the SAME feature never collide and it never fires; **`guards/session.ts`** tracks `activeSessions.get(workspacePath)` in an in-process `Map`, so it is blind to any other process by construction; and **`guards/file-lock.ts`** gives cross-process *write* safety — it stops two writes from interleaving, it does not tell anyone that somebody else is working here. The normal pattern — read state, carry the notes forward, write them back — therefore persists a display artifact as genuine content, and `tw_update_state` accepts it verbatim with no guard firing (verified by the lane). Repeated across hops it accretes. **The irony is load-bearing, not decorative**: this is a self-inflicted instance of exactly the contamination class E86 was filed to stop, arriving through the read path instead of the write path, and E92's new marker widens it rather than introducing it. **Not a regression from that cut** — the lane was explicit that the `…[truncated]` half predates it — which is why it was escalated rather than folded in under a cap. **Fix shapes**: mark synthetic entries structurally (a sentinel prefix the write boundary rejects, which reuses E86's machinery), or strip them on the read side of the write path so a carried-forward array is cleaned before persisting. | E138 | **A CORRECT PRECEDENT ALREADY EXISTS A FEW FIELDS AWAY, measured 2026-09-17**: `scope_decision_why` has a **2000-char hard cap that fails LOUD** — the L-INIT lane hit it writing E102's feature state, the write was rejected outright with a clear error, and the author simply shortened. Same write path, same class of free-text field, **opposite overflow behaviour**: one refuses audibly, the other truncates in silence. Whoever cuts this row or E135 should start from that contrast rather than designing a policy from scratch. **The `pending_notes` read view drops the TAIL, so what a role loses is systematically the newest and most load-bearing material** (found by Session C live, 2026-09-17, as NEW-1 — a different defect from E135 despite the shared origin, and both should be kept). Measured on the lane: sr-engineer wrote **13** notes (5113 chars on disk); `tw_get_state` returned **7**, with the truncation marker. **The five that did not come back were the last five** — the build result, the full test result, the pointer to the expected-red manifest, the boundary-compliance attestation, and the instruction *"do not mark these tasks complete"*. **That is precisely the material the NEXT role needs**, and one of the lost entries is an instruction whose absence causes a wrong action rather than merely a gap. The truncation keeps the earliest notes and drops the most recent, which inverts the usual value ordering of a handoff: a chain's oldest notes are context, its newest are the handover. **Distinct from E135**: E135 is that synthetic markers are indistinguishable from author prose on write-back; this is that the KEEP/DROP choice itself favours the wrong end. **Distinct from E92**: E92 established the cap is a read-time display truncation and added a whole-drop marker, so a reader now knows something is missing — this row is about WHICH things go missing. Fix shapes: keep the tail rather than the head, or keep both ends and elide the middle (the middle of a long note list is usually the least load-bearing), or raise the read cap now that E92 established nothing is lost on write. **Measured again 2026-09-22 (E132 lane), and this is the first time the size of the loss is on the record**: truncation fired on **four consecutive handoffs** at 4776, 3779, 3261 and 3098 characters against a 3000 cap — so between 3% and 59% of each note set was dropped, silently, at exactly the moment the next role was meant to read it. The lane compensated by re-carrying the facts in each dispatch brief, which is the workaround this row exists to remove. **A chain that loses handoff context at the handoff is a governance defect, not a cosmetic one** — recorded here rather than filed separately because it is the same defect this row already describes, now with numbers | P2 | E92 ✓ | ~2 (`tools/handoff-parse.ts` truncation policy + a qa-owned pin) | — |

Blast radius was nil — the field is transient, the author noticed within one turn and corrected it with a second write, and nothing persisted. But the write passed **all 33 gates**, and the only thing that surfaced it was the author saying so. The reviewer's phrasing is the finding: **detection that depends on candour is not detection.** **Why it matters beyond bookkeeping**: a fabricated APPROVED is not inert — the coordinator's auto-routing reads the verdict and would advance the chain to qa-engineer on it, which is Constitution §3.2's builder-is-not-judge boundary failing at the state layer rather than the prose layer. A tolerated pass is never silent — it prints `NOTE: tag-at-HEAD — tolerated N governance-bookkeeping commit(s) ahead of tag vX.Y.Z` before `OK: tag-at-HEAD`. Options (b) and (c) rejected at cut time — (b) inverts step 13a, whose commit carries the closing write naming the tag it would precede; (c) cannot change an exit code, so the only prose that unblocks is prose telling the actor to hand-judge Check 1, which is the regression E9 exists to prevent. Known residual, filed not fixed: Check 6 computes `releaseSha` from `git rev-parse HEAD`, so once the tolerance fires it queries CI about the bookkeeping commit (**L-RELTOOL-NEW-1**, Wave 3; WARN-and-continue is that check's contract). Wave 1.5 added step 13a, which commits governance bookkeeping **in the same session, after the tagged release commit** — so HEAD advances past the tag. **The SOP's nominal order avoids this**: 9a is marked *pre-closing-write* and runs before 13a. **This repo's actual workflow does not.** The last three releases (v3.109.0, v3.110.0, v3.111.0) all had release-engineer stop before every remote operation and hand the remote phase to the human — by which time 13a's commit already exists locally, so 9a necessarily runs with tag != HEAD. **Consequence is a hard stop, not a warning**: `content/skill-release-engineer.md:262` routes any `verify-release` FAIL to `status: Blocked`, and the script exits 1 (both confirmed). Waves 2-7 each end in a release; each would halt. **Measured live on v3.111.0**: five of six checks green, the sixth failing purely because `8084f12` (the release commit, correctly tagged) is not `3ef72c2` (the bookkeeping commit). The release itself is sound; the invariant is what became unsatisfiable. **Note the shape**: 13a was added to close E84's gap and opened this one — the second time in that release a new step created a new problem, both surfaced only by execution. Fix options: teach the check to tolerate a tag followed only by governance-bookkeeping commits; or order 13a's commit before the tag; or make the SOP handle the deferred-remote-phase pattern explicitly. Sharpened by what happened on the same release: `dc3b1d3`'s CI was genuinely RED on its first run and only went green on a no-code-change re-run, so the two commits' verdicts are not interchangeable even in practice. **Scope note (why P2 and not P1)**: in the SOP's nominal order 9a runs while `tag == HEAD`, where Check 6 is correct; the false OK is reachable only on a post-13a run. It becomes mandatory-path if releases revert to the pre-v3.112.0 practice of running 13a before 9a. Fix: resolve the release sha from `refs/tags/v${version}` (the `rev-list -n 1 <tag>` Check 1 already does), falling back to HEAD only when no such tag exists. | E148 | **A gate that exists to catch fabricated timestamps randomly fires on genuine ones, turning any seed-then-write test into a coin flip** (measured live on the v3.112.0 release CI, run 35308236600, 2026-09-18: 2097/2100, green on re-run with no code change). `gates/stamp-provenance.ts` arms `STAMP_PROVENANCE_SUSPECT` when the prior on-disk `last_updated` matches `HAND_AUTHORED_STAMP_RE = /T\d{2}:\d{2}:00\.000Z$/` — seconds `00` AND milliseconds `.000`. The gate's stated premise is that the server's own `new Date().toISOString()` carries millisecond entropy and is "overwhelmingly unlikely" to produce that shape. **Overwhelmingly unlikely is ~1/60000, not zero.** `seedAndRead` (test/e28-shrink-warning.test.mjs) lets the server stamp a real `now()`, so the following `runUpdate` is rejected with the refusal envelope and the test's `JSON.parse` throws `Unexpected token '⛔'`. Hit J1 this time; every seed-then-write test across the suite is equally susceptible, times two Node versions per CI run. Latent since `1826840` (**2026-07-14**, E18) — roughly two months. **The cost is not the debugging time, it is the reflex**: the working disposition for this failure is "re-run CI", which is also the wrong disposition for a real red. The v3.112.0 coordinator did exactly that (after diagnosing it, but the precedent is set), and Waves 3-7 are all multi-lane, so the frequency rises as the reflex hardens. Fix direction: give the seed a fixed, entropy-bearing stamp instead of letting the wall clock decide. **Test ownership is qa-engineer's (§2)** | P2 | none | ~2 (test seed helper(s) + a qa-owned pin; the gate itself is correct and should not be weakened) | **DONE — shipped v3.113.0** (2026-09-18, merge c29ac1b): `test/e148-seed-stamp.mjs` gives every seed-then-write test control of its own stamp; 16 test files converted plus `test/e148-stamp-provenance-seed.test.mjs` as the acceptance proof. **The gate is untouched and no stamp-override argument was added to `writeHandoffState`** — weakening either is the regression E18 exists to catch. Acceptance is deliberately NOT "J1 went green" (J1 was already green 59999 runs in 60000): SEED-1 forces a seed onto the suspect shape and shows the gate reject on 100% of runs, SEED-3 shows a forced-safe seed survive deterministically. Suite 2100 → 2104. | E163 | **Step 9a can only ask CI about a commit that is already pushed, so a red CI is always discovered after the tag and the GitHub release are public and immutable** (measured 2026-09-22 on v3.115.0, the first release where this actually fired). Step 8 pushes the branch, pushes the tag and runs `gh release create`; only then does step 9a reach CI ground-truth. On v3.115.0 the first five 9a checks passed, CI came back `failure` for head `3313c40` (run `35699511268` — the E162 defect), and 9a correctly refused the closing write and refused to claim Released — but by then `main`, tag `v3.115.0` and the GitHub release all existed publicly, and the SOP's own rule (never delete a published tag, never `git tag -f`) correctly forbids withdrawing any of them. The only remaining route was a fix-forward patch, so **a red CI burns a version number every time, and the check that would have prevented it had already run and passed everything it was able to see**. **The reorder is nearly free**: CI keys on the commit sha, not on the tag, so pushing the branch alone is enough to obtain ground truth — push branch → wait for CI green on that sha → then push the tag, create the release, and run 9a unchanged. The polling loop and its budget already exist (`DEFAULT_WAIT_SECONDS` 480s, `AGC_VERIFY_CI_WAIT_SECONDS`), so the cost is one wait the SOP already pays, moved earlier; nothing new has to be built to know the answer sooner. **Distinct from the existing CI-ground-truth family, which is why this is its own row**: E78 (a green run belonging to a different commit), E80 (WARN-and-continue swallowing the normal case) and E82 (the 600s default colliding with the agent-harness Bash ceiling) are all about whether the check is *correct*; this row is about *when it runs relative to publication*, and every one of those fixes leaves the ordering exactly as it is. Open question, and it decides the shape: whether the tag-push/release-create half becomes its own numbered step or stays inside step 8 behind an inserted gate — which in turn decides whether E141's tag-at-HEAD tolerance wording needs amending, since the tag would now be pushed after a CI wait rather than in the same breath as the branch **SECOND HALF, MEASURED 2026-09-22 AFTER v3.115.1 SHIPPED — nothing ever asks CI except step 9a, so a red `main` survives indefinitely between releases.** The v3.115.0 red was not introduced by that release: `main` had been red for **six consecutive commits** — `2b85d73` (the Wave 3 `feat/e132-lane-registry` merge), `a5a5e79`, `56aa775`, `0c516bc`, `1927ffa`, then the release commit `3313c40` — every one of them failing on the *same* E162 `pathspec 'main'` signature (verified by reading the oldest and the newest failed run's logs, not inferred). Four docs commits landed on a red `main` and nobody noticed, because step 9a is the only thing in the entire system that ever queries CI, and it runs once per release. So the ordering fix above shortens the window from 'after publication' to 'before the tag', but a `main` that went red weeks ago is still first discovered at release time. **Scope judgment**: closing this *at release entry* is cheap and in-scope — a pre-flight CI query before any release mechanics, refusing to start on a red `main`, reusing Check 6's existing poll and sha-resolution. Continuous watching *between* releases is a different mechanism (CI notification/branch-protection config, not agc SOP prose) and is deliberately NOT claimed here; if wanted it is its own row. Filing both halves together because they share one remedy family — ask CI earlier, and more than once — and because splitting them would cut the same file twice. | P1 | none | ~1–2 (`content/skill-release-engineer.md` step 8/9; possibly a pre-tag mode in `scripts/verify-release.mjs`) | **DONE — shipped v3.116.0** (2026-09-22): both halves. First half — step 8 split into 8a (commit + push BRANCH only, reversible) / 8b (mandatory `node scripts/verify-release.mjs --ci-check --strict` CI gate) / 8c (tag + push, irreversible from there); sub-lettered, not renumbered, so step 9 and every existing cross-reference stay intact, and 8b's prose separates a host-level command-timeout kill (silence — re-run) from a printed verdict (the only thing that escalates). Second half — new step 2a queries CI for current HEAD before any bump and refuses to start on a red `main`, lenient by design because nothing irreversible exists yet at that point. Both reuse one shared `evaluateCIGroundTruth({ sha, strict, fails })` factored out of Check 6 in `scripts/verify-release.mjs`, reached via a new `--ci-check [--strict] [--sha]` mode; Check 6 itself is behaviourally unchanged (`strict: false`, still resolves the released sha from the tag per E147). Two new Escalation Routes rows back both gates (7 → 9). Continuous CI watching between releases stays deliberately unclaimed, as the row scoped it. Follow-ons E164 and E165 filed from this feature's own review and qa; both left open. |
| E164 | **DONE — shipped v3.117.0** (2026-09-24): step 8b's post-kill re-run is sequenced (lower `AGC_VERIFY_CI_WAIT_SECONDS` below the host timeout) and bounded by the `fix_try` cap -> Blocked/human. Original row: **Step 8b's retry guidance is unbounded, and the one strict FAIL that would end the loop is unreachable on exactly the hosts that need it** (found by the code-reviewer at E163 round 2, 2026-09-22, and concurred by that feature's qa; scored non-blocking by both because it never publishes). E163's step 8b tells an operator that a host-level command-timeout kill is silence rather than a verdict, and to re-run rather than escalate — correct, and it converges in ≤2 attempts for a CI run that actually completes (completion is a fixed wall-clock instant set by 8a's push, so successive attempts tile the same timeline). **But for a CI run that hangs, it does not converge at all**: the `AGC_VERIFY_CI_WAIT_SECONDS` budget-expiry path is the only thing that prints a strict `FAIL:` for a never-completing run, and on any host whose command timeout is below that budget (default 480s; the measured agent-harness default is 120s) the process is killed before the budget can expire. So the printed-verdict path is unreachable, the Escalation Routes row keys on a printed verdict, and the operator livelocks with no escalation route left. **Safety direction is correct — it can never publish a tag — which is why it is P2 and not P1.** **Cheapest fix, per the reviewer**: advise in step 8b that the budget be set *below* the host's command timeout, which converts an unobservable livelock into the existing printed-verdict path with no new prose semantics and no code change. Worth checking whether the same reasoning applies to step 9a's own poll, which shares the budget knob **RIDE-ALONGS FILED 2026-09-23 (Wave 4 integration, `NEW-TICKETS.md` L-CONTENT section) — E164+E167 is the next `content/` lane, so these ride its single cut:** **(a) L-CONTENT-NEW-1**: `content/skill-coordinator-lite.md:21` still quotes the pre-E110 5-column cut header (no `touches`). A literal-minded PM would then see every lane pair overlap and serialize everything. Append "(governance bookkeeping excluded)" and re-measure the skill-pm floor. This matters before Wave 5, which runs four parallel lanes. | P2 | E163 | ~1 (`content/skill-release-engineer.md` step 8b; possibly the same note at 9a) | — |

Original row: **E163 split step 8 at the reversible/irreversible seam but left a reversible action inside the irreversible sub-step** (found 2026-09-22 by the release-engineer executing v3.116.0 — E163's own first live exercise, on itself). Step 8c bundles `git tag -a` (local, reversible, undoable with `git tag -d`) together with `git push origin vX.Y.Z` (remote, irreversible) into one sub-step placed entirely after the 8b CI gate. But the local phase legitimately wants the tag created before the gate runs — the tag object is part of what the local phase produces — and 8c offers no seam for that. The executing release-engineer resolved it correctly by creating the tag locally and holding only the push behind 8b, noting that **8b's own prose already names the push, not the tag object, as "the one irreversible act in this file"** — so the prose is right and only the sub-step boundary is one notch too coarse. No harm occurred and the gate's protection was never weakened. **Fix is a choice, not a bug hunt**: either split 8c into tag-create / tag-push, or add a sentence permitting local tag creation before 8b. The second is cheaper and matches what already happens in practice; the first is more honest about where the seam actually is. (1) Counting is fixed in code: Wave 5.1's hand-written `n=$(grep -c … \|\| echo 0)` produced `0\n0` on an empty file (grep -c prints 0 AND exits 1), so every integer comparison errored and the watch never fired — seq 1 sat unread ~15 min until the human asked. | E192 | **DONE with E178a 2026-09-27 (merge `81056a6`): `content/skill-integrator.md` lets the integrator dispatch a fresh-context generic read-only verifier (D10, no dedicated template), five constraints cited by number.** **FOLDED into E178 2026-09-25 (human decision) — ships with E178, do not cut separately.** **The integrator's lane-layer verification runs inline in the one session that must see every lane, so its context fills with verification output instead of coordination** (filed 2026-09-25, human-directed, during Wave 6.1b dispatch). Stage 5a of the interim integrator SOP (`.claude/commands/integrator.md`) has the integrator itself re-run the full `npm test` on each lane's committed branch, read `git diff --stat`, `git show` evidence files, and check `pending-tickets.md` — and 5b repeats the full suite on the integration branch. Mailbox traffic is cheap (measured: the longest lane so far, e179 at hop 10/10, is ~20KB both directions), but full-suite output (2500+ tests), diffs, evidence files and ledger greps (`docs/backlog.md` 708KB, `tasks.md` 548KB) are not; with N concurrent lanes they accumulate in the single session whose job is to hold the cross-lane picture, and auto-compaction then drops coordination detail first. **Direction**: the integrator MAY dispatch a fresh-context verification subagent (Task) per lane report for the 5a checks (and for 5b's integration-layer full suite), receiving back a structured result only: branch sha + `main..<branch>` commit list, `diff --stat` against the manifest's owned list with out-of-bounds files named, each cited evidence file's verdict line + task id, `status --porcelain` of the worktree, full-suite pass/total with the full log written to a scratch file (not returned), and the `pending-tickets.md` / `## Applied` / root `NEW-TICKETS.md` check. **Constraints the cut must keep**: (1) the verifier is read-only — no git writes, no `tw_update_state`, no edits, no test authoring (§2); the close/return decision stays with the integrator; (2) its report is itself a claim — the integrator re-checks the cheap anchors itself (`git rev-parse <branch>` equals the reported sha; greps the saved log's summary line for pass/total) before `close`; (3) verifier runs are serial, never parallel — concurrent full suites flake (E182), same rule as today's lane-layer reruns; (4) the verifier must be a fresh context that was never any lane's session (§3.2, the same bar the integrator already meets); (5) it is NOT code-reviewer or qa-engineer — those are judges that write verdicts and state, whereas this is claim-vs-branch verification; the cut decides between a generic read-only agent and a dedicated template under `templates/claude-code-agents/`. **Placement**: the formal integrator SOP is E178 (v4 Wave 7, `content/skill-integrator.md`); filed post-v4 by the §2.4 default because no remaining wave depends on it — folding it into E178 instead is a human decision (it would avoid writing the formal SOP twice, and Wave 7.1 is the first wave since Wave 5 with concurrent lanes). | P3 | E178 | ~2 (`content/skill-integrator.md` or `.claude/commands/integrator.md` stage 5a/5b; optional `templates/claude-code-agents/` verifier template) | — |

The first parse is `finish --shipped` on primary, after the branch is already merged to `main` and pushed, so the fix costs an extra lane commit + a second integration merge (`7a2625a`); had the lane session been gone, the fix would have had to be improvised on main. **Silent half**: `agc check`'s orphan detector (`bin/agc-init.mjs` ~:861) calls the two-argument `parsePendingTickets(text, lane).entries.length > 0`; a block that fails to parse is skipped into `errors`, not `entries` — verified: a file whose only block is malformed returns `entries 0, errors 1` — so a stranded branch carrying only malformed findings is reported as having no unapplied tickets, and its findings are lost without a warning. **Direction** (cut decides): (1) a lane-side validation entry point — e.g. `agc check` (or `lane-status`) parses the current lane's `.current/<lane>/pending-tickets.md` and prints every `errors` line, and `docs/lane-protocol.md` §4/§6 makes it part of the pre-report checklist; (2) the orphan detector treats `errors.length > 0` as "has unapplied findings" (warn, never silence); (3) optionally the integrator SOP's stage-5a check parses instead of `git show`. Do not relax the parser (finish refusing a malformed block is correct — the gap is how late it runs). | P3 | E179 | ~3 (`bin/agc-init.mjs` check path, `tools/lane-ticket-allocation.ts` if a validate helper is exported, `docs/lane-protocol.md`; tests by qa) | — |

It scans tracked plus untracked non-ignored file contents and file names. It reports only file, line and category, never the matched text, and printed paths are masked on keyword substrings. Evidence: `review_reports/review_T-E234-01.md` (code review, round 3 approved), `qa_reports/review_T-E234-05.md` (qa, round 1 pass, covers T-E234-01..05). Post-merge rescan with the integrator's local keyword source: 0 real hits. Original row: **No mechanical check catches a NEW instance of E231's banned classes once that rule ships** (filed 2026-09-28, split out of E231's cut per the fine-grained-ticket default — same gap E232 describes for the pre-existing leaks, but forward-looking). Add an advisory scan (e.g. an `agc check` pattern-based check) for the classes E231 bans — internal URLs/work-item links, third-party client/project codenames, design-tool file keys, credentials, absolute local paths/usernames — over tracked files, warn-only (false positives are acceptable since it never blocks). Depends on E231 shipping first so the scan's patterns target the rule's finished wording rather than a moving target. (filed by lane `e231` as `E231-NEW-1`; source: pm cut, specs/e231-info-hygiene-rule.md Decision §4 (split out per the fine-grained-ticket default)) | P3 | E231 | — | — |
| E235 | **DONE — merged to main 2026-09-28 (lanes e235a `31941cc`, e235b via `integ/e235b`)**: handoffs now store `prd_path` relative to the workspace and resolve it back to an absolute path on read; old absolute values still read unchanged; a stored value that resolves outside the workspace is dropped with a one-line warning; no schema version bump (the format tells the two forms apart, documented in `docs/schema-versions.md`). The fan-out manifest worktree column is written relative to the primary checkout and `render` turns it into an absolute path; an absolute cell still passes, with a warning from `validate`. All 32 existing protocol-produced files were rewritten in place with no annotation (human ruling), together with 5 role-authored files the earlier cleanup missed because the local path appeared in an encoded session-directory or temp-directory form, and the adopter-project-name cleanup ruled under E240. A lane's own handoff written before the merge still carries the old form; the integrator fixes it at close-out. Evidence: `qa_reports/review_T-E235A-07.md` (qa, round 1 pass, covers T-E235A-07..08) with `review_reports/review_T-E235A-01.md` (code review, approved in round 2); `qa_reports/review_T-E235B-06.md` and `qa_reports/review_T-E235B-01.md` (qa pass) with `review_reports/review_T-E235B-02.md` (code review, approved in round 1). Original row: **Committed handoffs and fan-out manifests carry absolute local paths that expose a username** The server writes absolute local paths into machine fields that get committed: a lane handoff's `prd_path` (and any other absolute-path field that persists into `.current/<lane>/handoff.md`, which lanes commit on their branch). The fan-out manifest format has the same problem, because its worktree column requires an absolute worktree path (`specs/fanout-*.md`). Both leak a local username into tracked history, which is the class the information-hygiene rule bans. The rule itself treats a value a tool's input schema requires verbatim as protocol, so the fix belongs in the server and the manifest format, not in role behaviour: for example, store paths relative to the primary checkout or the lanes root, and resolve them at read time. Cover both surfaces in one fix. (filed by lane `e231` as `E231-NEW-2`; source: e231 lane cut pre-review with the integrator, 2026-09-28) | P2 | E231 | — | — |
| E236 | **A rejected qa PASS write still appends a PASS section to the evidence file** The handoff write path records the qa_review text into `qa_reports/review_<task>.md` before it runs the expected-red check. When that check rejected the qa write (the report heading did not match the exact anchor), the evidence files had already gained a timestamped PASS section, and one file was created from nothing. The durable record now shows a PASS the server never accepted. Fix: run every rejecting check before any evidence write, or roll the evidence append back when the write is rejected. Pin it with a test that sends a write the expected-red check rejects and asserts the evidence file is unchanged. Example in this lane: `qa_reports/review_T-E231-04.md`, section timestamped 17:08:38Z. (filed by lane `e231` as `E231-NEW-3`; source: e231 lane qa hop, 2026-09-28 (coordinator confirmed from the committed evidence files)) | P2 | none | — | — |
| E237 | **Tasks completed after a PASS cannot reach the handoff's completed list, which leaves drift that cannot be cleared** qa's PASS write recorded only the tasks qa built. The builder's two tasks were then closed with the task-completion tool, which updates `tasks.md` but not the handoff's `completed_tasks`. A same-state PASS write to add them is rejected (no PASS to PASS transition), so the drift check keeps reporting "tasks marked done that the handoff doesn't mention" with no sanctioned way to clear it. Two fixes are needed: the qa SOP should say the PASS write lists every task in the feature, not only qa's own; and the task-completion tool (or the sync tool) should reconcile the handoff after a PASS. (filed by lane `e231` as `E231-NEW-4`; source: e231 lane closeout, 2026-09-28) | P3 | none | — | — |
| E238 | **A verify-release timing test fails under ordinary machine load, so an unrelated commit can show a red suite** (filed 2026-09-28 by the integrator while integrating lane e231). The test for the CI-wait-disabled path (`test/verify-release.test.mjs`, the "wait set to 0 performs exactly one gh call" case) asserts the whole run finishes in under 5000 ms of wall-clock time. With a parallel lane's test suite running on the same machine (load average ~6) it took 6807 ms and failed; neighbouring cases in the same file slowed to ~4 s each. It passed three times in isolation and on a full-suite rerun, and the commit under test changed only documentation. The assertion's intent is "no poll sleep happens", which the existing single-gh-call counter already proves; the wall-clock bound adds only a load-sensitive failure mode that parallel lanes make routine. **Second instance, same day (integrating lane e106):** the lane-registry scaling case in `test/e132-lane-registry.test.mjs` (asserts a 15-lane read is slower than a 2-lane read, best of 3 trials) failed at 35 ms vs 42 ms under load average ~5 and passed five times in isolation — a tens-of-milliseconds comparison is swamped by machine noise. **Third instance (same day, pushed main):** a prompt-dispatch case in `test/teamwork-lite.test.mjs` (asserts the composed prompt still carries the constitution) failed once while the MCP server was being reconnected in the same session — the double-injection guard saw an injected-constitution marker — and passed 3/3 in isolation and on a full rerun; it is environment-coupled rather than timing-coupled, but the same class: a full-suite result that depends on what else the machine is doing. **Fourth instance (same day, verifying lane e232):** the lane-registry presence case in `test/e132-lane-registry.test.mjs` (reads real sibling worktrees under a subprocess time ceiling) failed once under load average ~6 while another lane ran its suite, passed 3/3 alone and on a full rerun. **Fifth instance (same day, pushing the E235 manifest):** the same `test/teamwork-lite.test.mjs` prompt-dispatch case failed, and this time it also failed 3/3 in isolation. The cause is environmental, not load: the SessionStart hook writes a freshness marker into the real primary workspace, the test spawns the server against that workspace, and for two minutes after any session starts there (for example, when lane sessions are opened) the server decides the constitution was already delivered and omits it. After the marker went stale the case passed 3/3 alone and the full suite went green. Fix for this case: point the test at a temp workspace (or clear the marker in its setup) so it no longer depends on sessions starting elsewhere. Fix both the same way. Fix: drop the wall-clock bound, or raise it well above any poll interval and keep the call counter as the real check (qa-owned) | P3 | — | ~1 (one test file, qa-owned) | — |

Original row: **agc init run from a repo subdirectory writes root-anchored exclude rules that miss the files it just created** With cwd in a subdirectory of the repo, `agc init --artifacts=local` (or the default) writes `/.current/`, `/tasks.md`, `/qa_reports/`, `/review_reports/` anchored at the repo root, yet the scaffold lands in `sub/.current/` and `sub/tasks.md`. It still records "local" and reports the rules as added, while `git status` shows those files untracked-but-not-ignored — the accidental commit path the flag exists to close. Fix shape: prefix the rules with the workspace path relative to the repo root (or refuse / warn when cwd is not the repo root), and make `agc check`'s drift predicate use the same prefix. No E106 AC covers this case. (filed by lane `e106` as `E106-NEW-1`; source: code-reviewer, E106 review round 1 (review_reports/review_T-E106-01.md)) | P2 | E106 | — | — |

[ ]`; `agc check` and `agc eject` treat the same segments the same way through the one shared predicate; both messages and `docs/install.md` describe the character class instead of listing four characters, and print the offending segment with control characters shown as visible escapes; `--artifacts=repo` is unaffected; evidence: `review_reports/review_T-E243-03.md` (code review, round 1 approved), `qa_reports/review_T-E243-03.md`, `qa_reports/review_T-E243-04.md` (qa, round 1 pass, repro-red against the base recorded). Original row: **agc init local mode does not refuse a backslash or control character (CR/LF) in the workspace path, so the written exclude rule silently misses the scaffold** A backslash is a gitignore escape character: a directory named `a\b` yields the rule `/a\b/.current/`, which git reads as `/ab/.current/`, so the created files are untracked but not ignored and `agc check` stays silent. A CR/LF in a directory name would split one rule into several exclude lines. The current refusal set is only `* ? [ ]` (fixed verbatim in three user-facing messages), so widening it is a copy change as well as a code change. Not reachable on Windows, where the backslash is the path separator. | E246 | **Lane mailboxes are never removed, so a later lane with the same name inherits stale messages** (filed 2026-09-28 by the integrator after the feedback-leftovers fan-out). The integrator creates `<lanes-root>/_mailbox/<lane>/` (`to-integrator.md`, `to-lane.md`) at dispatch, but nothing removes it at teardown: `content/skill-integrator.md` stage 6 lists `agc feature finish`, deleting the integration branch and checking the worktree list, and `docs/lane-protocol.md` has no mailbox close-out step; `agc feature finish` moves `.current/<lane>/` and removes the worktree and branch but does not know the lanes-root. The files are outside the repo, so nothing leaks, but a future lane that reuses a name (a follow-up ticket for the same id) starts with old messages in place — and if the integrator only runs `mkdir -p` instead of truncating, `mailbox-watch.mjs` baselines count them, so old mail is treated as already read or as new. The durable record already lives in the fan-out manifest's Decisions table, so the mailbox contents need not be kept. Fix options: (i) add a stage-6 step to the integrator SOP — after each lane's `finish --shipped`, delete `<lanes-root>/_mailbox/<lane>/` (content-only, needs the golden/budget refresh); (ii) have `agc feature finish` accept or derive the lanes-root and remove that lane's mailbox itself (code + a qa-owned case); (iii) both — tool does it, SOP names it. ### Recommended execution order (2026-08-10, post-v3.96.0 — E44/E49 shipped; amended 2026-08-11 post-v3.97.0 — E50 shipped, E53/E54 filed; amended again 2026-08-11 post-v3.97.1 — E51 shipped, E56/E57 filed; **amended 2026-08-27 — E96 filed and placed at order `0b` by human priority call**, from the coordinator's audit of its own host system prompt; amended again 2026-08-27 post-v3.104.5 — E94 + E97 shipped, and **E99** (E96 option (ii), split out at intake: it needs a schema bump) + **E100** (`host` is never declared, so every real workspace composes the LEAN coordinator — filed from E96's review round 1) placed at orders `13k` and `0d`, the latter a coordinator proposal awaiting a human priority call; Written for a fresh implementation session. Every row's intake classification is stated, per the coordinator SOP's *Cheapest-Compliant-Path Intake* — the classification is a proposal, not a pre-approval; the cut still needs a human nod unless it clears the `cutApprovalAutoTier` threshold (currently armed with conservative defaults: `maxFiles` 2, `maxPriority` P3). E52 (P2) and E54 (P3) don't clear the auto-tier either; none of E52/E53/E54/E56/E57 do (E56 is P3 but its `~1` estimate is a spec file, and auto-tier's `allowDesignArmed`/`allowSchemaChange` defaults are beside the point — it is the `maxFiles` 2 / `maxPriority` P3 pair that decides, so re-check per cut rather than assuming). E53/E54 were filed from E50's own review findings the same way E50 itself was filed from E49's; E56/E57 are new as of the post-v3.97.1 amendment and come from E51's review and build gates respectively. All were inserted to interleave with the carried-forward rows rather than superseding their reasoning.

The 2026-09-08 audit's own recommendation was to file it separately and run it first. |

Numbered `0` rather than renumbering the tail, per this file's standing convention (the rows below are referenced by number in its own notes). The human's rationale, recorded rather than re-derived: artifact isolation and the feature lifecycle govern **how every future feature in every adopter workspace is run**, so anything sequenced after it may have to be redone under whatever the design pass decides — it is a foundation row, and foundation rows go first regardless of severity label. Note the two axes are separate and only one moved: this row's `P2` is a SEVERITY label and is unchanged; what changed is its position in the development sequence. Precedent for the split — E63 is `P3` at order 9a and E70 is `P3` at 8e, both sequenced by dependency rather than by P-level. **The trade-off this promotion accepts, recorded for the record and not to reopen it**: order 8d (E69 + E71) was the previous front-runner and remains the only open row whose SHIPPED artifact is already wrong at HEAD — E69's two fence sites mis-render in the real `tw_switch_role` dispatch path, so every release-engineer dispatched today receives broken SOP text, and that stays true for however long E73 runs first. E73 fixes nothing currently broken; it prevents a class of adopter breakage before it is paid for at scale. That is a legitimate ordering preference — but the cost is real and should be visible here rather than discovered during the next release. Execution guidance from the original 8g row still holds in full: run the design pass **READ-ONLY** and let it size the cut. The mechanical half (an ignore rule at init) is an afternoon; the expensive half is the constraints with silent failure modes — harvest-before-teardown, and keeping `docs/backlog.md` plus `tasks.md` outside the disposable worktree. All seven constraints and every figure in the row were independently re-verified against the tree on 2026-08-17, so the design pass starts from audited ground and should not re-measure. **Sequencing amendment 2026-08-17 (coordinator recommendation; the human approved recording it) — order 8d (E69 + E71) is recommended to run BEFORE this row, and order 5 (E57) second.** This does NOT reopen the order-0 promotion: the foundation-row argument stands and E73 remains the main line. The insertion argument is a SIZE argument, not an importance one — this row's first step is a READ-ONLY design pass over seven constraints plus two still-undecided cells (`specs/` and `tasks.md` in the third amendment's exclude set), i.e. thinking work that 8d cannot be slotted inside; whereas 8d is one file's fence move plus its qa-owned render test, which fits any single day. Meanwhile 8d's cost accrues daily AND replicates: E69's two fence sites mis-render in the real `tw_switch_role` path, so every release-engineer dispatched before it lands receives broken SOP text, and E71(a) and (b) both re-fired at v3.102.1 with (b) gaining a second site. E57 is placed second on the same reasoning applied to a different axis — it is the only open row carrying a security label and the only one whose cost grows silently (five HIGH advisories waived and counting, with no natural stopping point), and it starts as a cheap coordinator-direct triage. **Also recorded: order 8f (E72) should sit BETWEEN this row's two slices**, not after both — the original 8g reasoning that ledger trustworthiness is upstream of ledger storage was never refuted, only skipped by the promotion, and it bears directly on constraint (2)'s harvest question (what is worth extracting depends on whether the ledger can be trusted). Recommended full order: 8d, E57, this row's design pass, slice 1, 8f, slice 2, then the P3 prose batch (E70 + E61 + E62 + E74 in one release — E43 left this batch and shipped alone as v3.104.0 once its intake was reclassified to a mini-chain) |

Sequenced here, above the P3 prose tail and above E52/E54/E47, for two reasons the rows above do not share: the current default is **actively wrong rather than merely stale** (every `agc init` commits the governance ledger into a shared repo, and `CLAUDE.md` itself says the cross-machine coordination that would justify it does not exist), and it silently breaks the one parallel-work route the coordinator SOP already recommends — a tracked `handoff.md` hands every new worktree a poisoned lease at birth. Kept BELOW 8f (E72) because that row is about whether the ledger can be trusted at all, which is upstream of how the ledger is stored. Do the design pass read-only and let it size the cut: the mechanical half (an ignore rule at init) is an afternoon, but shipping only that half leaves the two expensive constraints — harvest-before-teardown, and keeping the backlog outside the disposable worktree — undocumented, and both have silent failure modes (deleting the review evidence before the human PR review; a done-mark that dies with the worktree so the next intake re-picks the same ticket forever). Both halves of the worktree mechanism are already **measured, not assumed** — `info/exclude` is shared across worktrees via `$GIT_COMMON_DIR`, and ignored artifacts do not block `git worktree remove` without `--force` — so the design pass starts from verified ground and should spend its budget on the lifecycle rules, not on re-proving git behavior. **Amended 2026-08-17 — constraint (4) (code comments citing artifacts that will no longer exist) was FOLDED IN rather than filed as its own row**, deliberately: the answer to "may code cite `E`-numbers" is *determined* by constraint (3) (where the backlog lives), so splitting them would let two mutually-determining decisions evolve in separate tickets and contradict each other. It also re-sizes this row from ~4 to ~6 and adds a second deliverable shape — a scaffolded reference check plus one `content/skill-code-reviewer.md` line — so the design pass now has to decide enforcement layer as well as lifecycle. Measurement is already done and recorded in the row (37 path citations / 658 ticket-id occurrences / 103 constitution-referencing comment lines, of which 30 are literal `§ <n>` citations — across 77 non-test source files); the design pass should not re-run it. **Every checkable claim in this row was independently re-verified 2026-08-17** after the human flagged an elevated error rate; that audit corrected three figures (the `§` count's label, `driftBaselineIds` 500+ → 558, and `tasks.md` 323+ → 833, the last of which had been a diff hunk header misread as a file length) and re-ran constraint (5)'s `git worktree remove` claim as a real experiment rather than a recollection — the first attempt at that test was itself invalid, having used a TRACKED file that reported as modified rather than untracked. Everything else held. **Second amendment 2026-08-17 — constraints grew 4 → 7 and constraint (3) was CORRECTED, all from one human restating the workflow back in their own words.** That readback is what exposed them, and it is worth noting as a method rather than an accident: (3) had asserted a file must live OUTSIDE the worktree and, one clause later, that it should be per-worktree — an internal contradiction that survived being written, reviewed and committed, and that only surfaced when someone tried to *execute* the sentence. Same discovery mechanism as E64→E66→E67→E71, now reproduced on a backlog row instead of an SOP. The row also caught its own hardcoded "three constraints" going stale after a fourth was folded in — the E70 count-drift class, in this row's own prose |

(ii) and (iii) are not agent work at all: §6 forbids the force-push and the repo deletion is a GitHub-console operation. | **PARTIAL — prevention half shipped v3.107.0** (done-mark owed from that release and backfilled here at v3.110.0, not shipped by it): prevention (c) landed as `checkResearchBinaries()` in `bin/agc-init.mjs` (commit `d505878`, absent at `v3.106.0`, pinned at `test/agc-adapters.test.mjs`) and (a) landed `af33f3b`; the ticket row recorded this while this order row never received the mark, and its chain was left stranded at `next_role: pm`. **Still OPEN:** the residual-history facet (option (ii), the `git-filter-repo` rewrite) is human-owned — it needs a force-push Constitution §6 forbids an agent to run, plus a GitHub Support purge request. ## A1 — Registry pattern for tool & prompt registration (P1) — DONE 2026-07-07
- **What:** `index.ts` is 1436 lines; adding a tool requires touching three
  places (`ListToolsRequestSchema` list, zod schema, `CallToolRequestSchema`
  dispatcher case — per CLAUDE.md), and prompt registration is an 11-branch
  if-chain (`index.ts:382-402`). The three registration sites can drift.
- **Fix:** each `tools/*.ts` exports `{ name, schema, handler }`; `index.ts`
  iterates a registry to build the tool list, validate args, and dispatch.
  Same for prompts: a `Map<promptId, buildFn>` replaces the if-chain.
- **Owner:** /teamwork (cross-module refactor; pm→architect→sr→reviewer→qa).
- **Risk if skipped:** every new tool/prompt re-pays the triple-registration
  tax; a missed site ships a tool that lists but doesn't dispatch (or vice
  versa).

## A2 — Split `evidence-file.ts` into per-gate modules (P1) — DONE 2026-07-08 (via A10)
- **Done:** folded into and shipped as part of feature `gate-registry` (spec
  `specs/gate-registry.md` + architecture `specs/gate-registry-architecture.md`;
  v3.46.1). `tools/evidence-file.ts` (994 lines) drained to shared read/write
  plumbing only (path helpers, section slicing, cell parsers); its 15
  `has*`/`check*`/`validate*` predicates moved verbatim (no behavior change)
  into `gates/qa-review.ts`, `gates/code-review.ts`, `gates/visual.ts`,
  `gates/scope-decision.ts`, `gates/cut-approval.ts`. Every caller
  (`tools/handoff-orchestrator.ts`, `prompts/build.ts`) retargeted to the new
  import paths. Import DAG acyclic (`evidence-file.ts` has zero `gates/`
  imports). See A10 below for the fold-in rationale.
- **What:** `tools/evidence-file.ts` (994 lines) has grown from "file-mode QA
  evidence write/check" into gate-central: review, code-review, visual
  baselines/evidence, design-mode arm signal, scope-decision, cut-approval —
  10+ `has*` predicates in one file, each consumed by a different
  `transitions.ts` gate.
- **Fix:** extract a `gates/` directory, one module per gate (e.g.
  `gates/qa-review.ts`, `gates/code-review.ts`, `gates/visual.ts`,
  `gates/cut-approval.ts`, `gates/scope-decision.ts`), aligned with the gate
  trigger points in `tools/transitions.ts`. `evidence-file.ts` keeps only the
  shared read/write plumbing.
- **Owner:** /teamwork (pure refactor but wide import surface; sr→reviewer→qa).
- **Risk if skipped:** the file keeps absorbing every new gate; predicates
  entangle and a change to one gate's parsing silently affects another's.

## A3 — Validate constitution span-strip markers at build time (P2) — SUPERSEDED by A9
> A9 (compose-not-strip) eliminates the fence-stripping mechanism entirely, removing the
> unbalanced-marker failure class this validator guards. Implement A3 only if A9 is rejected.
- **What:** `prompts/build.ts` strips constitution spans via markdown fence
  markers (`stripChainOnly` / `stripRationale` / `stripDesignOnly`). A typo or
  unbalanced marker in `content/constitution.md` silently changes the
  governance text agents receive — no error, no test failure tied to the
  marker itself.
- **Fix:** add a build-time check (pattern: `scripts/check-version.mjs`) that
  asserts every strip marker is paired, spans are non-empty, and each strip
  mode produces non-identical output where expected. Wire into `npm run build`
  / `npm test`.
- **Owner:** /teamwork (qa-engineer owns the test; small script).
- **Risk if skipped:** governance text corruption is silent and only surfaces
  as agent misbehavior in downstream workspaces — the hardest failure class to
  trace back.

## A4 — Strip version/origin tags from governance text at build time (P1) — DONE 2026-07-06
- **What:** Nearly every rule in `content/constitution.md` and `content/skill-*.md`
  carries inline provenance tags — `(v3.26.0, R5)`, `(B10)`, `root cause C1`,
  `§四#7`, references to retrospectives the executing agent cannot read. For the
  agent consuming the prompt these are pure noise: they change no behavior, cost
  tokens on every dispatch, and add cognitive load.
- **Fix:** extend the existing strip infrastructure in `prompts/build.ts` — either a
  new `<!-- origin:start/end -->` fence or a regex pass that removes `(vX.Y.Z…)` /
  root-cause-code tags at bundle time. Source files keep full provenance for
  maintainers; agents receive clean normative text. Estimated 5–10% token saving
  per role prompt.
- **Owner:** /teamwork (build.ts + content markup; qa verifies bundle output).
- **Risk if skipped:** every dispatch pays the tag tax; rules read as archaeology
  instead of instructions.

## A5 — Error-code contract test: content ↔ code (P1) — DONE (commit 3360c68)
- **What:** Governance prose asserts server behavior by name —
  `VISUAL_PROVENANCE_MISSING`, `CUT_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, `BASELINE_MANIFEST_MISSING`,
  etc. Nothing prevents those claims drifting from what `tools/transitions.ts` /
  `tools/evidence-file.ts` actually throw.
- **Fix:** a test that (a) extracts every `SCREAMING_CASE` error code mentioned in
  `content/*.md`, asserting each exists in code; (b) reverse direction: every
  gate error code in code is mentioned in at least one content file. Cheap; no
  behavior change. Interim guard until A10 makes the relationship generative.
- **Owner:** /teamwork (qa-engineer owns the test).
- **Risk if skipped:** doc rot — agents follow prose describing gates that no
